# Internals

Contributor notes on formats and protocols that code and names cannot carry. Source files stay comment-free (`nitpicker/no-comments`).

## Diff engine (`core/`)

- **Custom analyzers.** A `{name, command, args?}` config entry runs with the analyzed checkout as working directory, so a relative command resolves inside each side. It prints findings JSON on stdout and exits 0; stdout goes through the same parser as built-in analyzers, and a non-zero exit or invalid JSON becomes a tool error.
- **Exit codes.** New violations win over tool errors so CI can tell "violations" from "broken"; `overallStatus` reports tool health first.
- **Gradle failures.** The bounded error shows the Gradle failure block before earlier output. Failed runs also retain unfiltered stdout and stderr in a private `nitpicker-gradle-failure-*` system temporary directory; the error links to `gradle.log`. These logs outlive snapshot/report cleanup until the user or operating system removes them. They contain the project build output, so inspect them before sharing. A log write failure reports why saving failed alongside the original build error.
- **Tool-error paths.** `runAnalyzers` rewrites tool-error messages (scheduler errors and `tool-error` findings) of a run on a temporary snapshot (`core/diff/snapshot-root/`): a path under the snapshot's project root becomes project-relative, the root itself becomes `base` or `head`. A run on the live project keeps its paths.
- **Repository-local git variables.** `runCli` starts by removing every variable `git rev-parse --local-env-vars` names from the process environment (`detachLocalGitVariables` in `core/diff/git/environment/local-variables.mjs`), so its git calls and every analyzer run against the repository of their working directory. A hook sets them: in a linked worktree a pre-commit hook gets `GIT_DIR` and `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, and git in the temporary base and head worktrees would otherwise read the hook's worktree and index. `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` and `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` stay, because they carry `git -c` settings, as git keeps them when it enters another repository (`prepare_other_repo_env` in git's `submodule.c`). The function returns the removed values.
- **Analyzer environment.** Analyzers never see where the GitHub Actions job summary lives (`HOST_CI_OUTPUT_VARIABLES`): vitest and Stryker would append their own reports to it, and caught mutants read as test failures.
- **GitHub output.** `writeGithubOutput` (`core/diff/reporting/github/emit.mjs`) runs after the console summary of a comparison (not of a shard worker), of `nitpicker aggregate` and of `--all`. `githubOutputPlan` reads the environment given to the CLI: the summary goes to `GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` (appended; a write failure is a stderr warning, never an exit code), the annotations to stdout when `GITHUB_ACTIONS` is `true`, and `NITPICKER_GITHUB_OUTPUT` is `on` (annotations anywhere) or `off` (neither). Both render from the report objects of `reporters.mjs` and `snapshot/reporters.mjs`, not from the console text: `diff-summary.mjs` and `snapshot-summary.mjs` build the Markdown from `markdown.mjs` (tables of counts by tool and rule, the first `LISTED_FINDINGS` enforceable findings as links to `GITHUB_SERVER_URL/GITHUB_REPOSITORY/blob/GITHUB_SHA/<project folder>/<path>#L<line>`, tool errors, `summary.policyChanges`, `summary.notices`, and the exception route: the entry quoted from the first finding message that ends with the exception sentence, which is cut from each listed message), `workflow-commands.mjs` the `::error file,line,col,title` lines. Paths are prefixed with `git rev-parse --show-prefix` of the project so a project in a subfolder annotates the right file. Escaping follows the workflow-command spec: `%`, `\r` and `\n` in data, and also `:` and `,` in properties. At most `MAX_ANNOTATIONS` (10, GitHub's limit of error annotations per step) are written, tool errors first (at most two, skip notices last), then a `::notice` counts the rest. The composite action in `action.yml` only prepares the checkout and the cache and runs the CLI; the summary and annotations come from the CLI. The inputs and outputs listed in `docs/ci.md` are generated from the descriptions and defaults of `action.yml` by `scripts/action-inputs.mjs`, run by `npm run gen:checks` with the other generated files.
- **Cache stores.** A store keeps opaque text under a hex key, is synchronous and never throws: `get` returns undefined on any miss or failure, `put` returns whether the value was stored. The local directory is `NITPICKER_CACHE_DIR`, else `node_modules/.cache/nitpicker`, else `nitpicker/cache` in the Git common directory when it is writable (`resolveCacheDirectory`); with no local directory and no remote store, `resolveCacheStore` adds the warning that the base side runs every time. The HTTP store does `GET`/`PUT` of `<baseUrl>/<key>.json` with an optional `Name: value` auth header, and stays off after its first non-miss failure so an unreachable server costs one timeout. Reads try local, then remote (copying a hit locally); writes go to the remote store only with `writeRemote`.
- **HTTP cache worker.** Exit codes: 0 success (GET prints the body), 3 not found, 1 any other failure. The comparison is synchronous, so each request runs in a short-lived `node` child that does the asynchronous fetch: `<GET|PUT> <url> <timeoutMs>`, auth header in `NITPICKER_CACHE_AUTH_HEADER_VALUE` (never argv). S3 answers 403 for a missing key when the caller cannot list the bucket, so 403 counts as not found. The test HTTP server lives in a worker thread for the same reason: the stores block the calling thread.
- **Findings cache key.** Base commit, project, analyzer, tool version, configuration hash, head policy (exclude, layout, exceptions, thresholds), legacy inventory path and nitpicker's code fingerprint. The fingerprint is computed once at module load from relative paths and contents: `package.json` (including version and pinned dependencies), shipped `.mjs` files in `core/` and `shared/`, and shipped files in `languages/` (including non-JavaScript rules and tool configs). Tests, fixtures, test helpers, README files and Python bytecode are left out, as in the package file list. Installation paths and Git metadata do not affect it, so a checkout and its packed install share keys. The policy is keyed explicitly because layout and threshold changes rebuild an analyzer without changing its file-based hash. Entries carry their own key, so a misnamed entry is a miss. Only clean reports are stored, and cached analyzers still run when an uncached one depends on them. A cache-hit progress line names the local directory (or remote cache) and the `--no-cache` escape hatch.
- **Publishing a base snapshot.** `cache publish` analyzes HEAD (typically the new tip of `main`) and stores clean reports under the keys later comparisons use when HEAD is their merge base, using HEAD's own policy.
- **Comparison.** Findings split into new, resolved, unchanged and changed-metric. With `touchedCode`, an enforceable finding of a strict rule inside touched lines is new even though the base has it, unless it carries `ratchet.existingCodeOnly` (an ESLint finding inside a named function): then only a line in `inserted` makes it new, and `changed-lines.mjs` leaves out of `inserted` an added line it pairs, by word similarity, with a removed line.
- **Red base.** An analyzer report on the base whose tool-error findings are all `tests-failed` or `tests-failed-skipped` (`shared/rules/finding-helpers.mjs`), with at least one `tests-failed`, while the head report of that analyzer has no tool error, is a red base (`core/diff/comparison/base-red/`). Only a test run that executed and named a failing test emits `tests-failed`; a load, collection or compile failure, a nameless non-zero exit and a timed-out run emit `tests-not-run` (TypeScript, Python) or the `gradle-failure`/`maven-failure` of the preset, and stay tool errors. Red-base findings become `notes` (written to `notes.json` and the JUnit, SARIF, text and console reports, merged across shards by `aggregate`, never a tool error), the other base findings of the analyzer stay, and an analyzer the scheduler skipped on the base because of a red-base analyzer (CRAP behind coverage) is compared with no base findings, without the skipped label; the `tests-failed-skipped` notes are dropped in favour of the `tests-failed` ones. `unknownOnBaseFor(failures, touchedCode)` builds the `unknownOnBase` predicate of `compareFindings`: `isTestResultFinding` (`TEST_RESULT_RULES` in `core/diff/comparison/base-red/unknown-base.mjs` plus any `mutant-*` rule) and a path under a failing module, where a failure's `symbol` is the module directory (absent or `.` means the whole analyzer). A head finding of those rules with no base counterpart is new when `changedByDiff` says so (its file is added, or its `location.line..endLine` overlaps an added or edited range of the file, or contains a deletion hunk of it; a finding without a location only for an added file), else unchanged. When `touchedCode.testsRemoved` (`core/diff/git/patches/test-removal.mjs`: a test file with more removed than added lines, or a deleted one) there is no predicate and a `tests-removed` note is added, so those findings are new as on a green base. Ratchet holding does not apply to them, so `crapFindings` takes the function's `endLine`. Adapters keep the non-test findings of a red base: Python returns them with the `tests-failed` tool error (`test-runs/test-failures.mjs`, `testsFailOnBase` text on the base); Gradle (Java and Kotlin) reads a failed base run as a test failure only when the fresh test reports name failing tests (`failingTestsOf`, per project, any `Test` task) and, having run the base with `--continue`, runs again with `-Pnitpicker.testsFailed=<project dirs>`, which disables every checked test task of those projects, with their per-task `nitpickerPit<Task>`, the aggregate `nitpickerPit` and JaCoCo (the ArchUnit tasks still run: they read bytecode and never fail the build) and writes `testsFailed: true` into their `manifest.json` (`red-base/tests-failed.mjs` adds one tool error per project, `symbol` its directory, or for the root project its own source and test directories from its manifest joined by `,` so the other projects keep their comparison); Maven needs no rerun: its base build ignores test failures, `collectTestResults({ tolerateFailures })` lists the failing tests of the module instead of throwing, that module skips JaCoCo and PIT and gets `testsFailed: true` in its manifest and one `tests-failed` error with its relative directory as `symbol` (the root module: its own source and test directories, as in Gradle); TypeScript and JavaScript (vitest, jest, mocha, node:test) share `coverageFailureFindings`, which returns `tests-failed` when a test was named and ran, and `tests-not-run` when only files could not run or nothing was found (`core/diff/tests/policy/base-red/ts-red-base.test.mjs` runs that path with real vitest). `PIT` is ordered after `test` so a red suite fails at `test` and not inside PIT. A red head, or a base with any other tool error, stays a tool error.
- **Cycle identity.** Dependency cycles are `cycle:a>b>c|...`, starting at the lexicographically smallest rotation. A rename can change that rotation, so renamed cycles are re-rotated before comparing.
- **Moved class and module names.** Before comparing, base identities and symbols get the renamed paths and the qualified names of renamed Java, Kotlin, Groovy, Scala and Python sources (`core/diff/comparison/moved-names/`). A rename gives the name after the source root: the root is the part of the old path before the name, matched in the new path by its longest shared tail, so a move to another module still maps (a root with no shared tail, such as `src/main/java` to `src/main/kotlin`, maps when it keeps its length and its first segment; a flat layout moved under `src/` keeps its top-level package). Kotlin files also map their `Kt` facade class, Python `__init__.py` maps its package, and JVM descriptors (`Lshop/audit/Request;`, also after primitive parameters) map in slash form. The first qualified name of a finding on a renamed file is its subject: when no class of that name moved, it follows the file's package, which covers a Kotlin file declaring classes under other names. A name two renames send to different places is left as it is, and so is a name the path does not give (a default-package class, a top-level Python module, a Kotlin package that omits the common root). Ingesters keep the path out of hashed identities (Checkstyle hashes rule and message with line references removed, coverage gaps use `cov:<symbol>`), since the path is compared on its own.
- **Report hash.** Zero config keeps the original policy hash input; any override changes it.
- **Base coverage and policy changes.** `createBaseSetupResolver` (`core/command/policy-review/base-setup.mjs`) measures the base once, with its own config passed through `baseConfigCoveringHead(baseConfig, headConfig)` (`core/policy-changes/base-coverage.mjs`): `layout.sourceRoots` and `layout.testRoots` are the union of both sides (an unset side adds the default roots, `testRoots` without `e2e`, because a declared list runs every root it names and the base would execute end-to-end specs); `exclude`, `disable`, `layout.excludedGlobs`, `layout.entryPoints` and each rule's exception paths keep only the entries both sides declare; and each threshold is the lower of the two. A folder the change brings under the gate is then read on both sides, so its existing findings stay unchanged, and neither a widened nor a tightened policy turns old findings new; the facts read from the head checkout come from the head policy (`withHeadCheckoutFacts` in `languages/typescript/preset/head-policy.mjs`: `conventions`, `generatedFiles`, `frameworks`, `react.enabled`, `react.iconPackage`, `react.major`, `react.styling`, `react.autoCleanup`, `i18n.enabled`, `layout.colocatedTests` when the head checkout has no test root, for the Java and Kotlin presets `spring` (the build files name `org.springframework`, `javaHeadFacts` in `languages/java/adapters/facts/head-facts.mjs`) and `kotlin` (the effective formatter, `kotlinFormatterOf`), and, when the base config reads the project Prettier config, `tools.prettier.projectConfigDigest`, and when it reads the project ESLint config, `tools.eslint.projectConfigFile`, `tools.eslint.projectConfigDigest` and `tools.eslint.projectConfigMissing`; for `tools.jscpd`, `tools.knip` and `tools.dependency-cruiser` when the base config lists `"project"`, the head's captured `projectConfig`, `projectConfigDigest` and `projectConfigError` under the base's own precedence, or the head's settings when the head captured nothing (`headCapturedFacts` in `languages/typescript/preset/project-tools/project-tool-policy.mjs`)), as they do for a base analysed with the head config. The base config is read with `readConfigFile` (schema checks only, so a base that `rejectUnreadableSources` or `rejectUndeclaredPreset` would reject is still compared); one that does not load is compared as `{}`. The policy-change lines compare the configs as written. `readPolicyScope` (`core/policy-changes/policy-scope.mjs`) lists both snapshots with `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` and keeps the code files of the head preset's extensions wherever they are (`code`, `baseCode`), the head files also in the base (`keptCode`, `keptPaths` with their folders), the root sets of each side (`rootSets`), `basePathOf` (a head path mapped back through the run's renames, folders through directory moves), `existsInBase`, `presetRequired` (`presetToDeclare`), for the TypeScript preset every tsconfig of each side with its `checkJs` (`projectTsconfigs` and `checksJavascript` in `languages/typescript/preset/modules/tsconfig-files.mjs`: each `tsconfig.json` outside `node_modules` and the exclusions, plus the configs they reference, followed transitively), `productionReach` and `blobOf` (the git blob id of a head file). `productionReach` is computed once, on first use: one `reachableModules` walk (`module-resolution.mjs`, unlimited depth) from the head layout's source files, the `main`/`bin`/`exports` targets of every tracked `package.json` (`packageEntryFiles`) and the `layout.entryPoints` files, through hidden files, mapping each reached file to the root importer the finding names (`productionReach` in `production-imports.mjs`, specifiers read with `ts.preProcessFile`). For Python the same walk is the import scan of the coupling rules (`pythonProductionReach` in `languages/python/adapters/analysis/reach/python-reach.mjs`: `collectImports`, `importEdges`, parent packages of a reached module) from the production modules the head `exclude` does not hide; for Java and Kotlin it is `jvmProductionReach` (`languages/java/builds/reach/source-reach.mjs`) over the `.java` and `.kt` files, from the files under the main source directories that `exclude` does not hide: single-type and wildcard imports, qualified names in the code, and same-package types or Kotlin top-level declarations whose name appears as an identifier, comments and strings removed; a same-package or wildcard name that an explicit import (or its `as` alias) of the same simple name shadows does not count, and a top-level Kotlin `fun`, `val` or `var` without a receiver type counts only where its name is used without a receiver (not after a `.`). It reads sources, not class files, because the review runs before any build and the head classes do not exist yet. Presets without a walk have no reach. The resolver always calls `policyChangeFields(baseConfig, headConfig, scope)`, because code added under an unchanged config can still be hidden. Each part returns `{ loosened, changed }`, a loosening carrying its display `text`, its full `key`, the `files` it names and the guidance `kind` that selects its wording (`entry-point`, `exclusion`, `hidden-exclusion`, `threshold` and the like; the producer sets it next to the text, and `describe-policy-changes.mjs` `looseningKindOfSetting` does for the listed settings). `coveredPathChanges` (`paths/covered-paths.mjs`) counts an added glob as loosening when it covers a kept code path its base set did not cover: `exclude` and `layout.excludedGlobs` are one set (`keyedExclusions` in `paths/hiding.mjs`, over `layout.hiddenGlobs`), so a respelled glob covering the same files and a glob moved between the two keys (`<glob> moved from exclude to layout.excludedGlobs`) are only listed; exception paths are compared per rule (`exceptionPathsByRule`, the one collector, also used by `base-coverage.mjs`); `layout.entryPoints` over all code paths through `basePathOf`. A base root the head drops is loosening when a code file under it is under no head root of the same kind, and an added test root is loosening when a kept file that was production code in the base becomes test code (`testRootReclassifier`, the one reclassification, also used for hiding). Removed globs are listed as tightenings. `hiddenCodeChanges` (`paths/hidden-code.mjs`) decides per file. `hidingSets` (`paths/hiding.mjs`) builds one set for the exclusions, one per rule's exception paths (`exceptions`, `python.exceptions`) and, with a layout, one for test roots inside source roots; each has `hidesInHead(file)` and `baseHiders(basePath)`, the base globs or root of that set that hid the file's base path. For every code file the change adds (`existsInBase` false) or moves (`basePathOf` differs) that a set hides in the head: no base hider is `where the base config checked it`; base hiders that cover no `baseCode` file is `where the base hid no code` (dormant); otherwise, for the exclusions and test roots only, a file in `productionReach()` is `that production code imports`. An exception under an established glob never fails on imports, because the file is still checked by every other rule. The text lists three files; the key lists all. `buildNarrowingChanges` (`build-files/build-narrowing.mjs`) compares the entries `scope.buildSources` holds for each side: `roots` entries (a source set's directories, pytest `testpaths`), `excludes` entries (patterns with a `covers(file, pattern)` check) and `includes` entries (patterns with `inScope(file)` and `admits(file, pattern)`). The readers count only what nitpicker runs. `readGradleSources` (`languages/java/builds/narrowing/gradle-sources.mjs`, statements and block headers from `gradle-script.mjs`) reads `sourceSets` `srcDirs` assignment against `srcDir` addition per language, and `taskFilter` (`gradle-tasks.mjs`) the filters of the `test` task, the main and test compile tasks and the source sets (not `resources`): block form, receiver form (`test.exclude`, `tasks.test.get().enabled`, `tasks.getByName("test")`, `tasks.test.configure`), `withType` for `Test` and the compile types, nested only through `filter`, `options`, `useJUnitPlatform`, `useJUnit`, `useTestNG` and `onlyIf`; other `Test` tasks, dependency and JaCoCo excludes are not read. `readMavenSources` (`maven-sources.mjs`) reads the project and its `activeByDefault` profiles (an active profile may move `sourceDirectory` or `testSourceDirectory`): build-helper and Kotlin plugin directories, surefire excludes, `excludedGroups`, skip flags, `includes`, `groups` and `test`, failsafe excludes and skips only against integration-test names (`*IT`, `IT*`, `*ITCase`), compiler `excludes`, `testExcludes`, `includes` and `testIncludes`, and the `skipITs` and `skipTests` properties against integration tests only and only when a pom binds Failsafe, mirroring `maven-project.mjs`, which then runs `test` instead of `verify` (`maven.test.skip` is not read, and neither property counts against unit tests, because the build passes `-DskipTests=false` and `-Dmaven.test.skip=false`); a pom's filters, `pluginManagement` included, apply to the roots of every module in its folder subtree. `source-patterns.mjs` scopes them: a main filter to the main roots, a test one to the test roots, and surefire includes to the classes surefire runs by default. `readPythonSelection` (`languages/python/adapters/configuration/pytest/test-selection.mjs`) reads `testpaths`, `addopts` `--ignore` and `--ignore-glob`, and `do_not_mutate`, each counted only for the files pytest collects (`isCollectedTestFile`: `python_files` or the defaults, `conftest.py`, the Django names in a Django project); `-k`, `-m` and `--deselect` are not read, because `pytest-pins.mjs` strips them from the run and `uncollected-tests` reports the tests they hide. A `roots` entry loosens when a kept code file the entry's `applies` accepts was inside the base roots and is inside no head roots entry of any build tool that accepts it, so a Maven to Gradle switch on the same roots is quiet; an `excludes` pattern the base lacked loosens when it covers a kept file no base pattern covered, and is only listed otherwise; an `includes` entry loosens when a kept file in its scope was admitted by the base patterns (or the base had none) and is admitted by no head pattern. `generatedMarkingChanges` (`paths/generated-marking.mjs`) counts a kept code file that the head checkout marks as generated and the base did not, with the preset's own test (`isGeneratedJvm`, `isGeneratedPython`, `isMarkedGenerated` from `languages/typescript/preset/layout/generated-files.mjs`), because a marker makes every check skip the file. `toolConfigChanges` (`build-files/tool-config-changes.mjs`, readers in `tool-config-files.mjs`) fingerprints the files of each tool config the head opts into, and the base's only when the base opted in too, so an opt-in lists every file it starts reading (the `*.xml` files under `config/pmd/` and `config/checkstyle/`, the Kotlin detekt config and the `.editorconfig` files ktlint or ktfmt read (`kotlinStyleFiles`), the Prettier files with only the `prettier` key of `package.json`, and the ESLint flat config with the files it reads, `eslintConfigFiles` in `languages/typescript/preset/eslint-project/project-config.mjs`, read through `readEslintConfigFile` so files elsewhere in the repository count, and the knip files of `PROJECT_CONFIG_FILES` with only the `knip` key of `package.json`) and counts a changed or added file as loosening. `toolConfigText` (`tool-config-text.mjs`) normalizes each file first: XML without comments and layout, JSON parsed, YAML and TOML without comment and blank lines, JavaScript and TypeScript files as `ts.createPrinter({ removeComments: true })` prints them, other scripts without whole-line comments, ignore files without `#` comments, and an `.editorconfig` reduced to its preamble and the sections whose glob matches `.kt` or `.kts` files (ktlint, ktfmt) or the files Prettier formats, so an edit of comments, layout or another language's section is not counted; the project config is read from the head checkout for both sides, so a loosened config would otherwise pass unseen. `tsconfigChanges` (`paths/tsconfig-changes.mjs`) counts a base tsconfig with no head counterpart (`tsconfig removed: <file>`) and `checkJs` turned off in any kept one as loosening, and lists `checkJs` turned on. `listedChanges` (`loosenings.mjs`) adds newly disabled analyzers, thresholds above the base with `THRESHOLD_DEFAULTS` filling missing keys on both sides, and custom analyzers removed or changed (whole entry compared); added analyzers, re-enabled analyzers and lowered thresholds are listed. Every other changed leaf setting is listed as `path: base -> head` (`not set` when absent); `looseningKindOfSetting` marks the ones that check less, and names their kind: `preset` by the preset that runs (`typescript` when unset; `javascript` to `typescript` is not loosening, and a first preset is not loosening only when `scope.presetRequired` says the project must declare one), `react.enabled`, `i18n.enabled` and `python.importLinter` turned to `false`, `kotlin.formatter` turned to `none`, any `kotlin.detektConfig` change, and a `tools.<tool>.precedence` that ranks `"project"` higher (for `pmd`, whose layers are unioned, only dropping `"nitpicker"`; for `eslint`, whose project ignores, globals, settings and added rules apply with nitpicker first too, any added `"project"`; for `knip`, whose project ignores add to nitpicker's, listing `"project"` at all, and more so first). Engines, `python.typeChecker`, `python.formatter` and the like stay neutral. `policyLoosenings` is the subset that fails: `buildReport` adds one enforceable `nitpicker-config/policy-loosened` finding per entry (path `nitpicker.config.json`, identity the change) unless the run accepts it; `policyFindings` (`core/diff/reporting/review/policy-findings.mjs`) words it as the change, the guidance of its kind (`POLICY_GUIDANCE` in `policy-guidance.mjs`: one or two sentences on what the change does to the checking and how to undo it, so a setting never says to fix findings where that makes no sense) and the route for a person reviewing the change, and records the kind as `policyKind`. `policyChangeFields` returns the kinds as `policyKinds` (change text to kind), `run-diff.mjs` passes them to `buildReport`, and a finding without a kind gets a generic fallback. `guidance/guidance-coverage.test.mjs` drives one fixture per producer through `policyChangeFields` and fails when a loosening has no kind or no entry in `POLICY_GUIDANCE`, or an entry has no producer fixture. `policyDigest` hashes the sorted loosening keys and, for every file they name, its path and git blob id (`summary.policyDigest`; a loosening may carry `blobs`, file to blob id, which wins over `blobOf`: the Python tool configs are named by their path in the repository, which may lie above the project folder), so an acceptance covers exactly the loosenings and file contents a person saw; `results.mjs` falls back to the loosenings alone when no scope supplied one. `policyAcceptance` (`core/command/policy-review/policy-acceptance.mjs`) honours `--accept-policy-change` only when the CLI is interactive (stdin and stdout are TTYs, `withCliDefaults` in `core/cli.mjs`) and `NITPICKER_ACCEPT_POLICY_CHANGE` only when `CI` is set; an ignored one is written to stderr, and `summary.policyAcceptanceSource` names the route used. `summary.policyChangeAccepted` records an accepted loosening. The evals harness fails a case whose agent used either route or wrote a generator marker (`evals/detectors/policy/policy-shortcuts.mjs`). Only shard 0 (or an unsharded run) adds the findings, and `aggregate` accepts the `nitpicker-config` tool in a shard's entries, so a sharded run counts them once. `summary.json` carries `policyChanges`, `policyLoosenings` and `notices` as arrays of strings, `aggregate` copies them from the first shard (`summaryFields`), and `report.txt` prints them under "Policy changed in this change" and "Notices (not failing)". The console (`consolePolicySummary` in `review/policy-changes.mjs`) takes the unapproved changes from the findings, groups them by `policyKind` (the changes of a kind one per line, then the kind's guidance once), keeps the other changes after them, and ends with `Unapproved policy changes (nitpicker-config/policy-loosened): N`, so an agent can stop on the id, and the acceptance command with the run's digest; the findings themselves are not listed under the code violations, and the headline counts code violations and unapproved policy changes separately (`appendViolationSummary` in `summary.mjs`). Every policy line passes `printable` (`format/text.mjs`: terminal escapes, control characters and format characters such as bidi overrides removed, whitespace collapsed) because a change text can hold an exception `reason`, so a reason cannot add lines or recolor the output. Notices never affect the exit code. `outsideRootsNotices` (`languages/typescript/preset/notices/`) names head-side changed files outside every root when the preset is `typescript`; it skips declaration files, `*.config.*` files, dot folders, built-in and configured exclusions. The Python (`pythonOutsideRootsNotices`, outside `python.sourceRoots`, skipping tests, tool files such as `setup.py`, installed, build and documentation folders, generated files) and Java and Kotlin (`jvmOutsideRootsNotices`, outside `src/<set>/java|kotlin` and the directories the build files declare) notices share the wording of `shared/notices/outside-roots-message.mjs`; `noticeDescriber` picks the describer by preset.
- **Config base.** `base` in the config is refused when it is `HEAD` or `@` (`rejectHeadBase`), and when it resolves to the head commit and follows HEAD (`core/diff/git/repository/base-ref.mjs`): a hex SHA, an expression using `HEAD` or `@` (`HEAD~0`, `HEAD^0`, `@{0}`), or a name whose `--symbolic-full-name` is the checked-out branch (`main`, `refs/heads/main` while on `main`). Any other branch that points at the head commit, such as `origin/main` on an up-to-date `main`, is accepted: the head includes uncommitted changes, so that comparison still checks the working tree, and the default remote-branch base relies on it. An explicit `--base HEAD` is accepted for the same reason.
- **SARIF output.** `core/diff/reporting/sarif.mjs` renders the SARIF files and the reporters write them from one
  `{ name, render }` table per mode (`DIFF_REPORTS` in `core/diff/reporting/reporters.mjs`, `SNAPSHOT_REPORTS` in
  `core/snapshot/reporters.mjs`), which also gives `REPORT_FILE_NAMES` and `SNAPSHOT_REPORT_FILE_NAMES` and the list the
  cleanup removes. A comparison run writes two files: `report.sarif` has one result per new finding
  (`baselineState: "new"`), and `report-baseline.sarif` adds one per held finding (`"unchanged"`, the ones in
  `held-findings.json`). Code scanning reads neither `baselineState` nor `suppressions` (its SARIF support page lists
  neither, and `upload-lib.ts` in `github/codeql-action` does not read them), so it would open an alert for every held
  finding; `report.sarif` is the file to upload. Resolved findings are not written as `absent` results, since consumers
  such as code scanning read every result as a problem in the current code, and unchanged findings that are not held are
  in no report file, which also keeps a sharded run (`aggregate`, which only carries held findings) the same as a single
  run. `--all` writes one `report.sarif` from `findings.json`, with no `baselineState`. The hashed comparison key is
  `partialFingerprints["nitpickerIdentity/v1"]`, so consumers match findings across runs when lines move, and a finding
  has the same fingerprint in both modes. Tool errors become unsuccessful tool-execution notifications. Each entry of `tool.driver.rules`
  carries `shortDescription` and `helpUri` when the rule reference (below) has an entry for the rule. `run.columnKind`
  is `utf16CodeUnits`, the unit of a finding's `location.column` (`spec/findings.schema.json`): ESLint, TypeScript and
  the analyzers built on it, oxlint (its byte offsets are converted when read), pyright, PMD, detekt, ktlint and the
  Kotlin compiler count UTF-16 code units with a tab as one. Four tools count otherwise, and `withUtf16Columns`
  (`shared/ingest/columns/utf16-columns.mjs`) converts their columns after ingestion from the source line: ruff counts
  code points (`code-points`, in `readPythonFindings`); javac (Error Prone) expands tabs to every eighth column
  (`tab-stops`); Checkstyle takes the code-point index of the token, then expands tabs to its `tabWidth` over that many
  UTF-16 code units of the line (`checkstyle-tab-stops`, measured with Checkstyle 10.21.1; `withJavaColumns` in
  `languages/java/adapters/reporting/columns/java-columns.mjs` reads `tabWidth` from the project Checkstyle config when
  `tools.checkstyle.precedence` uses it, else 8), both in `collectJavaFindings`; and mypy, run with
  `--show-column-numbers`, counts UTF-8 bytes in 2.3 and a smaller, different number in 2.4, so `ascii-only` keeps its
  column only on an ASCII line it can read. A byte order mark is not part of line 1. Code scanning reads a relative
  `uri` as relative to the repository root and never reads `uriBaseId` or `originalUriBaseIds`, so each location's `uri` is
  repository-relative and percent-encoded: the project folder's prefix in the repository (`projectRelativePath` in
  `run-diff.mjs`, `relativeProjectPath` in `snapshot.mjs`, `git rev-parse --show-prefix` of `liveProjectRoot` in
  `aggregate-shards.mjs`) joined to the project-relative finding path, with `uriBaseId: "%SRCROOT%"` meaning the
  repository root, which `originalUriBaseIds["%SRCROOT%"]` gives as a `file://` URI ending in `/` for consumers other
  than code scanning (the `repositoryRoot` the reporters receive: `repositoryRoot` in `run-diff.mjs` and `snapshot.mjs`,
  `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` of `liveProjectRoot` in `aggregate-shards.mjs`). Without a git repository (or the
  programmatic `aggregateShardReports` call without `liveProjectRoot`) the results keep plain project-relative URIs with
  no `uriBaseId` and no `originalUriBaseIds`. `renderWithinUploadLimits` (`core/diff/reporting/sarif/sarif-limits.mjs`)
  keeps each file within code scanning's upload limits (`SARIF_UPLOAD_LIMITS`: 25,000 results per run, 10 MB gzipped):
  it renders the first 25,000 entries (new findings come first) and, when the gzipped text is still too large, the
  largest prefix that fits, found by bisection, and adds a `warning` notification naming the count and the JSON file
  that has them all. The tests validate both modes against the OASIS SARIF 2.1.0 schema
  vendored in `core/diff/reporting/test-support/sarif-schema-2.1.0.json`, taken from
  `sarif-2.1/schema/sarif-schema-2.1.0.json` of https://github.com/oasis-tcs/sarif-spec at commit
  `1742a253eb654bf8a4ed7c60eaef7412b2c23553` (sha256 of the file before Prettier reformatted it:
  `c3b4bb2d6093897483348925aaa73af03b3e3f4bd4ca38cef26dcb4212a2682e`). The schema is an OASIS work product, copyright
  OASIS Open, made available under the OASIS IPR Policy (RF on RAND Terms mode, see that repository's `LICENSE.md`); a
  JSON file cannot carry the notice, so it is recorded here. The loader in `sarif-schema.mjs` drops the draft-04
  `$schema` and renames the top-level `id` to `$id` so Ajv 8 (a dev dependency already installed through Stryker)
  compiles it. Its `uri`, `uri-reference` and `date-time` format checks are written by hand and approximate: they catch
  a malformed value, such as an unencoded space or a missing scheme, not every case RFC 3986 or RFC 3339 rejects.
- **GitLab Code Quality output** (`core/diff/reporting/format/codequality.mjs`). `codequality.json` is a Code Climate issue list: one issue per new finding (every finding for `--all`) with `description` (the message), `check_name` (`<tool>/<rule>`), `severity` (`major` for enforceable, `info` for descriptive), `location.path` from the repository root (the project folder joined to the finding path; the project folder itself for a finding without a path) and `location.lines.begin` (1 without a line). `fingerprint` is the SHA-256 of the project folder, a new line and the comparison key, so a finding keeps its fingerprint when lines move and two projects of one repository never share one.
- **The `enforcedBy` column.** `shared/test-support/enforced-by.test.mjs` compares every rule of `RULES`
  (`shared/policy.mjs`, `shared/project-rules/`, `shared/unused-api/`), for each of the five presets, with the adapters
  that emit it. `shared/test-support/rule-emitters.mjs` lists the source files of the preset's folder
  (`languages/typescript`, `languages/javascript`, `languages/python`, `languages/java`; Kotlin shares the Java pipeline
  and the Java column leaves out files with `kotlin` in their path; JavaScript adds the TypeScript adapters) plus
  `shared/hygiene`, follows their relative imports into `shared/`, strips comments (`//` and `/* */`, `#` in Python) and
  finds the rule by its id, by the key it has in `RULE_IDS`, `HYGIENE_RULE_IDS` or `PACKAGE_RULE_IDS`, or by the helper
  that builds it (`FUNCTION_STRUCTURE_LIMITS`, `mutantRuleId`); the catalog, message, exception and config modules do
  not count. A rule marked enforced that no adapter emits, and a rule an adapter emits that is marked not enforced, fail
  the test, and so does a rule marked enforced for Kotlin but not Java that no file with `kotlin` in its path emits
  (other Kotlin claims can still be met by a Java file the Kotlin pipeline runs, or by a shared one guarded by a preset
  check). Each failure names the catalog file to fix. The only allowed second case is a rule the adapter emits that is
  switched off for a preset by a guard in the code (the Java and Kotlin facts run only the reuse rules, Go has no
  inheritance, JavaScript projects skip `unused-prop`, `unused-option` and `javascript-in-typescript-project`); the
  test lists each in
  `NOT_ENFORCED_ALTHOUGH_EMITTED` with the file and the pattern that proves it. `reports-docs.test.mjs` in
  `core/diff/reporting/` does the same for the report files: the lists in `docs/usage.md` and `docs/agents-snippet.md`
  must equal the reporters' lists, and every report file name in `README.md` or any `docs/*.md` page must be one the
  reporters write.
- **Base commit of a checkout.** `git.mjs` stages the checkout (untracked, non-ignored files included) in a copy of the index, leaving the user's index, branches and files untouched. The copy keeps the index's timestamps, so git's racily-clean check still re-reads a same-size edit made in the second the index was written.
- **Policy links.** Without `transform` the package-owned policy file is linked into the analyzed tree; with one (the layout changed) it is a rewritten copy, still derived from the head policy and never from the analyzed tree.
- **Shared build directory.** `runScheduler` creates one directory per side (`NITPICKER_SHARED_BUILD_DIRECTORY`, inherited by every analyzer process) and removes it when the scheduler exits. `buildOnce(key, build)` runs `build` in the first process to take `<key>.lock`; the others poll for `<key>.result` (or `<key>.failure`, rethrown so a broken build is not repeated). A lock whose owning process is gone (or an empty one left by a crash) is taken over by renaming it aside and re-checking it. A build killed by a signal records nothing, so a waiter builds instead; other failures are recorded. Without the variable, `buildOnce` just builds, so direct runs and tests are unchanged.
- **Temporary files.** Register before creating the file so it is removed on exit or interruption even if the runner's `finally` blocks never run.
- **Presets.** `preset` is explicit; there is no auto-detection. `rejectUndeclaredPreset` (`core/configuration/project-checks/preset-detection.mjs`) only refuses a config without `preset` when the source roots have no `.ts`/`.js` family file (best-effort: `ASSET_FOLDERS` such as `resources`, `static`, `public`, `assets`, `webapp`, `wwwroot` and `*.min.js` files are skipped, so a Spring app's bundled scripts do not count) and a Gradle, Maven or Python marker (`pyproject.toml`, `setup.py`, `setup.cfg`, `Pipfile` or `requirements.txt`) is at the project root, so the TypeScript preset never runs on a Java or Python project and ends in tool errors; the message names the preset (Kotlin when `.kt`/`.kts` files outnumber `.java` ones, skipping dot folders, `node_modules`, `build`, `target` and `out`). Injected analyzers win over the preset. Within the Java preset the build tool is detected (`detectJavaBuild`): Maven when the root has a `pom.xml` and no Gradle build file, or both but only `mvnw` and no `gradlew`; Gradle otherwise.

## Shared rules (`shared/`, `spec/`)

Findings follow `spec/findings.schema.json`. Identity (the part of the comparison key beyond rule and path) never contains line numbers, so edits above a finding do not change it.

- **Rule catalog** (`shared/policy.mjs`, `shared/project-rules/size-rules.mjs`). `policy.mjs` is the single source for the custom checks nitpicker adds on top of third-party tools: rule ids, default limits and the languages that enforce each rule (`RULES`: per rule, `limit` is the human text of the default threshold and `enforcedBy` says which languages enforce it today). The failure-message templates (`MESSAGES`, `GREW_MESSAGES`, `HELD_MESSAGES`, `EXCEPTION_HINT`, `renderMessage`) live in `shared/messages/messages.mjs`, which `policy.mjs` re-exports. Every language adapter reads it, and the custom-rules table in `spec/checks.md` is generated from it (`npm run gen:checks`). The size rules (the file limits `file-lines-limit`, `line-length-limit` and `folder-files-limit`, `folder-depth-limit` and the class limits Java, Kotlin and Python enforce), their defaults (`FILE_LIMIT_DEFAULTS`, which `policy.mjs` re-exports), their message templates and the Java line length live in `project-rules/size-rules.mjs`, which `policy.mjs` spreads into `RULE_IDS` and `RULES` and `messages.mjs` into the message tables. `project-rules/size-rules.mjs` imports nothing, and `policy.mjs` imports only from `shared/messages`, `shared/rules`, `shared/unused-api` and `shared/project-rules`, so any entry point can load the catalog. Like everything under `shared/`, neither may import from `core/`, `languages/` or a tool package (`shared/import-direction.test.mjs`).
- **Rule reference** (`shared/reference/`, `core/reference/`, `docs/rules/`). One entry per rule any preset can report (`{ id, tool, rule, anchor, page, findingTools, pattern, samples, analyzers, builds, summary, why, allowed, allowedIn, example, limit, forms, links, docs, exception }`), built by `buildReference` in `core/reference/build.mjs` from the catalogs that already hold the rules: `RULES`, `MESSAGES` and the rule-tool maps of `shared/policy.mjs`, the Java message catalogs, the PMD rulesets, `errorprone/checks.json`, the Spring architecture and configuration messages, the Kotlin and Spring review messages, nitpicker's detekt file and the Python `RULE_HINTS`. Where a rule's text lives in code, the entry renders the code's own message with placeholders (`<name>`) instead of keeping a copy, and where it lives in a guide table (`docs/java-preset.md`, `docs/kotlin-preset.md`), `shared/reference/build/guide-tables.mjs` reads the cell, so the reference cannot drift from either. `builds` is per preset and comes from `GRADLE_PRESETS` and `MAVEN_PRESETS` (`languages/java/adapters/gradle-presets.mjs`; the Kotlin preset needs Gradle). The exception of an entry names the config list and rule (`presetKeys` and `presetRules` where a preset uses another, `presetNotes` where it has none), and `reference-exceptions.test.mjs` runs the advice of every entry in every preset through `validateConfig`, with `samples` standing in for a pattern entry. The tools' own text comes from committed snapshots of the pinned versions: the ESLint inventory (`languages/typescript/reference/eslint-inventory.json`, refreshed with `npm run gen:rules -- --refresh-eslint`, checked by `eslint-inventory.test.mjs`), the ruff rules with the first paragraphs and the "Use instead" example of each rule's documentation (`languages/python/ruff/ruff-rules.json`, `--refresh-ruff[=<path>]`, which warns when that ruff is older than the snapshot's; `ruff-snapshot.e2e.test.mjs` checks the version and the rules against the ruff of the Python suite), and the PMD, SpotBugs, Checkstyle, detekt and Error Prone texts in `languages/java/reference/tool-text/` (`--refresh-jvm` fetches the pinned jars with Maven and reads them with `ToolText.java`; a test ties each snapshot's version to `nitpicker.init.gradle`). Only permissively licensed text is quoted: PMD (BSD-style), Error Prone (Apache 2.0), NullAway, ruff and ESLint (MIT) and detekt (Apache 2.0); SpotBugs and Checkstyle are LGPL, so their entries keep the pattern or check name, its category and the documentation link, and each page names its source and licence. An entry with no allowed form of its own prints "See the tool's documentation" with the tool's rule page, or the guide when the tool has none. `npm run gen:rules` writes one data file per page under `shared/reference/rules/` (what `nitpicker explain`, `nitpicker rules`, SARIF and the findings files read, so they ship with the package; each file keeps every distinct value once in `values` and each entry as a row of indexes, and leaves out `id`, `anchor` and `page`, which follow from the rest) and the pages in `docs/rules/` (`pages.mjs` lists them, `render.mjs` writes them already in Prettier's form, escaping the characters Markdown would read as emphasis or HTML outside code spans and links, and rewriting repository paths to links relative to the page). The generator-only sources (`core/reference/`, `shared/reference/build/`, `shared/reference/checks/`, `languages/*/reference/` and the snapshots) are left out of the package. `core/reference/generate.test.mjs` fails when a committed file is stale or a page reaches GitHub's rendering limit of 512 KB (the large catalogs have a page each for that reason); `reference-completeness.test.mjs` walks the catalogs and fails when a rule has no entry, so a new rule needs an entry in the provider that reads its catalog, and the e2e suites check that the `helpUri` of real findings names an anchor of the committed pages. An entry with `pattern` stands for every rule that matches it (for example `mypy/<error code>`); `findingTools` lists the other tool names a finding of the rule prints (`eslint-lint`, `eslint-errors`). The links are built by `docsUrl` in `shared/messages/docs-url.mjs` from the repository URL in `package.json`, at the `v<version>` tag in an installed package and at `main` in a git checkout (`docsRef`); a run reads that root and the reference data once (`referenceLinks` in `store.mjs`) and passes them to every report it writes.
- **Config and head policy** (`shared/config.mjs`, `shared/head-policy.mjs`). `shared/config.mjs` holds the `nitpicker.config.json` rules that do not depend on a language: the file name, the keys every preset honours (the feature folders of `layout` where the language registers the rules that read them), the glob checks and which keys each language's fact rules allow; `core/configuration/validate.mjs` adds the TypeScript-only keys and the preset gate, and `core/configuration/scopes/scope-settings.mjs` validates the `react`, `i18n` and `testConsole` scopes (`testConsole.allow` paths go through the same glob checks). The head policy is the config values that change what analyzers report (`exclude`, `layout`, `exceptions`, `thresholds` and, when set, `react`, `i18n`, `tools`, `testConsole` and `protocols`), written once to a file outside both snapshots so base and head runs see the same policy; its format and reader are language-neutral, and the TypeScript preset adds its layout defaults in `languages/typescript/preset/head-policy.mjs`.
- **Globs** (`shared/paths/glob.mjs`). `globMatcher(glob)` is what nitpicker itself matches paths with (`exceptions`, `exclude`, `layout` folder roots, `testConsole.allow`): it compiles `**`, `*`, `?` and `{a,b}` into a small automaton and builds its deterministic states lazily per character, so a test is linear in the path length however many `**` the glob has (the regular expression it replaced backtracked for seconds on ten `**/` segments over a 30-folder path). `globToRegExpSource(glob)` gives the same language as a regular expression source for the tools that take one (dependency-cruiser, mypy, Python tool excludes). The config check rejects a glob that can never match (a leading `./`, a trailing `/`, a `(?i)`-style flag, an unclosed `{`) in the `exceptions` of nitpicker's own rules, the `layout` folder roots and `testConsole.allow`; ESLint rule exceptions are left to ESLint, whose globs take character classes.
- **Project conventions** (`shared/conventions/`, `core/conventions/`). A convention is `{ id, label, options, optionLabels, fallback, files, measure(text, file) }`; `measure` returns the option one file follows, or `undefined`. `detectConventions` takes the files not under `exclude` (the CLI adds the layout's excluded folders, such as `dist`, `build`, `generated` and `target`), samples at most 400 per convention evenly over the sorted paths, counts one vote per file, and picks the plurality; a tie or no votes is `fallback` (`source: 'default'`), a `conventions` pin in the config skips measuring (`source: 'pinned'`). The CLI measures once the config, the analyzer selection and the base ref have been checked, so their errors come first and a run outside git fails as before: a comparison reads the merge-base commit through `git ls-tree -l` (regular blobs up to 512 KiB only, so generated files, symlinks and submodules never vote) and one `git cat-file --batch` by object id (so a branch cannot flip the choice); when every convention is pinned no git command runs, and re-measures on the working tree only the conventions the base had no files for; `--all` measures the working tree and `cache publish` the published commit (which later comparisons use as their merge base, so the keys match). Only choices that differ from the preset default go into the head policy, as `conventions: { id: option }` (omitted when empty, so default-style projects keep their policy and cache keys), also into the base's policy when the base has its own config, so both snapshots and the findings cache key see the same values. Presets list their conventions in `core/conventions/definitions.mjs`; a preset with none leaves the policy as it was. The TypeScript and JavaScript presets measure Prettier options (`languages/typescript/conventions/`) and add the choices to the `nitpicker` Prettier layer for code files, which then always runs through the layered Prettier API path. Short code says nothing about the print width, so a file votes a width only when it is the one value between its longest breakable line and the shortest call, array or assignment Prettier left wrapped (joined, that would have fit any wider limit), or 120 when at least two lines exceed 100. The measures skip comments and template-literal contents, and a file with no evidence casts no vote, so a project keeps the preset default rather than a guess. `prettier.jsxQuotes` and `prettier.bracketSameLine` (`conventions/measures/jsx-conventions.mjs`, TypeScript preset only: the javascript layout does not list `.jsx` or `.tsx` as source files, so the scope filter would drop every file) parse `.tsx`/`.jsx` with the TypeScript compiler (`typescript-syntax.mjs`): a quote vote is a `JsxAttribute` with a string initializer holding neither quote character, and a bracket vote compares the end line of the last attribute of a multi-line opening element with the line of its `>` (a self-closing element never votes). `prettier.jsonIndentation` and `prettier.yamlIndentation` (`measures/data-conventions.mjs`, `scope: 'formatted-data'`) are measured from the data files themselves and applied by `prettier-layers.mjs` to files with that extension: `useTabs` and `tabWidth` only, since nothing reliable can be measured for quotes or width in data files. The scope is the `prettierPatterns(layout)` list in `layout.mjs`, the same one the `prettier-format` check uses (so JSON at the root and under source and test roots, YAML under source roots only), minus generated and fixture paths. A `scope` on a definition makes `resolve.mjs` pass an `accepts` filter (`layout`: source and test files; `test-code`: test files plus files under `test-support`, `test-utils`, `testing` or `testkit`; `formatted-data`). A definition with `kind: 'names'` (`typescript.assertionHelpers`, `measures/assertion-helpers.mjs`) votes a list: `measure` returns evidence strings per file, `detectConventions` counts files per string, and `choose(counts)` (default: names counted in at least `minFiles` files) picks the sorted names; a pin is a list of plain identifiers, `sampleLimit` raises the sample size, and the policy carries the list. For assertion helpers a file reports `use:NAME` for the root identifier of the last statement of each `it`/`test` callback (heads: `it`/`test` with `only`, `skip`, `concurrent`, `sequential`, `each`, `for`, `fails`, `todo`, `runIf`, `skipIf`, called, tagged or plain) that contains no `expect`/`assert` call, and `def:NAME` for each function declaration or function-valued constant whose body calls `expect`/`assert`/`expectTypeOf`/`assertType` or throws; a name is followed when it ends tests in at least three files and is defined somewhere in the sample, so `checkIn(guest)` is not an assertion unless the project defines it as one. `defaults/eslint.config.mjs` appends the names to the `assertFunctionNames` of `vitest/expect-expect`. Main has no Jest assertion rule, so there is no second consumer. A hook file name (`use` plus a capital) is exempt from `typescript.fileNames` only when the file exports a function or constant named exactly like the base name, so that definition has `needsText` and the detector reads just those files (an unreadable one is treated as a hook). Two conventions are not Prettier options. `typescript.typeImports` (TypeScript preset only, `languages/typescript/conventions/type-import-style.mjs`) votes `separate` for a file with `import type` statements and `inline` for one with `import { type A }` specifiers (a file with neither, or with the same number of each, casts no vote) and sets the `fixStyle` of `@typescript-eslint/consistent-type-imports` in `defaults/eslint.config.mjs`; that rule accepts both forms and only reports a value import of something used as a type, so the choice only decides which form its fix would write, and the setting reaches the lint engines as ordinary rule options (oxlint plans from the ESLint config). `typescript.fileNames` (`file-name-casing.mjs` in the same folder, both presets) votes from the file path alone, so its definition is `nameOnly` (no file is read, and a file over the 512 KiB read limit still votes; `source.paths({ unreadable: true })` lists those) and `scope: 'layout'` (`resolve.mjs` passes `accepts`, which keeps only files the layout classifies as source or test files). The vote is the casing of the base name before the first dot (`kebab-case`, `camelCase` or `snake_case`); PascalCase names, names that fit every casing (`index`, `404`, `fr-CA`), reserved framework names, Remix route syntax (`[`, `]`, a trailing `_`), names starting with `_`, `+`, `$`, `@`, `(`, `#` or `~`, dot-files, `*.config.*`, names with a lowercase hash segment of 8 or more characters containing a digit, files the layout reports as generated (`isGeneratedPath`: dependency folders and the `generatedFiles` found by generator markers, which the vote skips through the `layout` scope and the rule skips by its own path and sibling listing), `*.graphql.*`, `*.gql.*` and `fixtures/` folders neither vote nor fail. `nitpicker/file-name-casing` (`eslint-plugin/rules/naming/`) takes the project casing as its option from the policy (`kebab-case` unless a convention says otherwise). When the file's folder holds at least three other voting files, their plurality replaces the project casing for that file (a tie keeps it), unless the config pins `typescript.fileNames`: `resolve.mjs` then records the pins as `conventionPins` in the head policy (even a pin equal to the default, which `conventions` omits) and the rule gets `pinned: true`; the rule lists the folder once per run with `readdirSync`, so it needs no extra policy data, and head and base each read their own checkout. It reports once at line 1, so it runs on changed files through the usual differential gate. The Python and Kotlin runners read the choices from the head policy file (`NITPICKER_POLICY`) like the TypeScript adapters.
  - **Applicability and sources** (`core/conventions/definitions.mjs`, `resolve.mjs`). A preset can also give `conventionStyle`, which returns `{ unused, project, note }` for the tools in use: `unused` maps the ids the formatter cannot apply to a reason (they are not measured or printed, and a pin for one is a config error), `project` maps the ids that a listed project tool config sets to `{ choice, from }`, and `note` adds a remark to a printed choice (black accepts both quote styles). A project value replaces a pin and a measurement for its id (`source: 'project'`, printed as "from your ruff config"), and a value outside the options (`tab`, `preserve`) is printed but never reaches the policy. Python: `python-style.mjs` picks ruff or black as the format check does and reads their config through `project-format-config.mjs`. Kotlin: `kotlin-style.mjs` reads the `.editorconfig` keys for `*.kt` (`editorconfig-values.mjs`), the ktfmt style the build names and the `WildcardImport` setting of the detekt config; only the tools whose `tools.<tool>.precedence` lists `"project"` first are read.
  - **Names** (`kind: 'names'`, `minFiles`, `sampleLimit`, `choose`). A convention that collects names instead of choosing an option: `measure(text, file)` returns evidence strings, the counts are per file, and `choose(counts, definition)` (default: every name seen in at least `minFiles` files) returns the followed names, sorted. The choice is an array (`[]` is the fallback, a pin is a list of identifiers) and `describeConventions` prints it as "final call of at least N of M measured files". `java.assertionHelpers` and `kotlin.assertionHelpers` (`measures/assertion-helpers.mjs`, with `languages/java/source/` extracting test methods and function definitions from the masked source) count `use:<name>` (the call that ends an enabled test method without a known assertion, in a `src/test`-style source set, and reads like an assertion: `expect...`, `ensure...`, `must...`, `should...`, `validate...`, `is...`, `has...`, `await...`) and `def:<name>` (a method or function in a test source set or a `test-support`, `test-utils`, `testkit` or `testing` folder whose body calls a known assertion or throws); a name is followed when it is used in at least 3 files and defined at least once, so production helpers such as `validate` or `isEmpty` are never learned. Known assertions are call names that are `assert`, `check` or `verify` followed by a capital or the end (Kotlin also `coVerify`, `expect`, `should`, Kotest infix `should...`, `confirmVerified`, `andExpect`, `forAll`, `fail`); `@Disabled` and `@Ignore` tests are skipped. `java.assertionHelpers` reaches PMD as the project's helpers appended to nitpicker's `extraAssertMethodNames` for `UnitTestShouldIncludeAssert` (`tool-config/pmd-layers.mjs`), on Gradle and Maven alike; `kotlin.assertionHelpers` feeds the Kotlin `test-without-assertion` rule.
  - **Java** (`languages/java/conventions/`): `java.formatter` (`measures/java-layout.mjs`, `formatterProfile`) votes per file only from evidence: tab-indented blocks are `none`, and otherwise the file needs a call the formatter wrapped after `(` (the Kotlin join measure with `wrappedOnly`, so the longest-line fallback of `java.lineLength` never votes for a formatter): two spaces at 100 and four spaces at 100 are `google`, four spaces at 120 `palantir`, 140 columns and two spaces at 120 `none`. The definition sets `minVotes: 3` and `strictMajority: true` (`shared/conventions/detect.mjs`: fewer voting files, or a winner with half of the votes or less, keeps the default `google`, whose indentation `java.indentation` still measures, `tab` included, from block-opening lines outside text blocks and comments; `withoutRawStrings` blanks text blocks). `java.lineLength` accepts any whole number of columns (`COLUMNS` in `shared/conventions/settings.mjs`: `valuePattern` and `unit` on the definition, used by pin validation, `appliedChoices` and the printed label; `kotlin.lineLength`, `python.lineLength` and `prettier.printWidth` carry it too, so a Checkstyle, detekt, `.editorconfig` or ruff width of 110 is followed instead of dropped). `build-style/` holds the project reading: `build-files.mjs` lists the build files of a conventions source (root and module Gradle files, version catalogs, `buildSrc/` and `build-logic/` plugins, every `pom.xml`, at most 200, none under `src/`, `node_modules`, `build`, `target`, `out`, `fixtures` or `testdata`), `build-comments.mjs` removes comments with a string-aware reader (`//` and `/* */` outside quotes for Gradle code, `#` for TOML; poms go through `parseXml`), `java-formatter-detection.mjs` returns `{ formatter, aosp }` from Spotless (`googleJavaFormat().aosp()`, `palantirJavaFormat().style("GOOGLE")`, `eclipse()` and `prettier()` in a `java` block as `none`), the palantir plugin, `fmt-maven-plugin` and the artifacts (most common declaration wins, a tie declares nothing), and `checkstyle-width.mjs` the `max` of a `LineLength` module (80 when it has none) with `parseXml`. `java-project-style.mjs` is the preset's `conventionStyle`: `project` maps `java.lineLength` to the Checkstyle `max` when `tools.checkstyle.precedence` lists `"project"`, and `derive(detected)` (a hook of `conventionStyle` that `resolve.mjs` applies after `project`) returns the formatter from the build file when it is not pinned and the choices the formatter fixes (`google` 100 columns, plus 2 or 4 spaces when it was declared, `palantir` 4 spaces and 120). A pin or Checkstyle width the formatter contradicts is a config error naming the source when the formatter is pinned or declared, and resolves the formatter to `none` (printed with the reason) when it was only the default or measured. The styles get a `source` (`{ paths, readMany }`) from `resolve.mjs`: the merge base when it holds Java sources (else the working tree), filtered by the policy's `exclude`, so a pull request cannot loosen its own limits by adding a formatter or editing the Checkstyle config; `kotlinStyle` and `pythonStyle` read the opted-in project configs (detekt, ktlint and ktfmt settings, `.editorconfig`, `ruff.toml`, `pyproject.toml` and the files they `extend`) from the same merge base through a config root, and only when a tool lists `"project"`: `lazyConfigRoot` (`core/conventions/style-snapshot.mjs`) writes the known config names of the project, its parent folders and the `extend` chains it follows, plus `kotlin.detektConfig`, into a temporary directory (made from the absolute temporary path) with an empty `.git` marker, so the tools' upward config search stops there. It copies shallowest files first, fails when more than 1000 style files match, leaves out names the system cannot hold (too long, `:`, reserved) with a printed note, and removes the directory when the conventions are resolved or when copying fails. Symbolic links are read through their target inside the project folder, in the merge base and in the working tree alike (`core/conventions/sources.mjs`). The formatter itself is detected from the same source without copying anything (`kotlinFilesFromSource`, `detectFormatterFromSource`), and a convention is unused only when neither the base nor the head formatter uses it, so a pull request that adds a formatter can keep its pin. A choice read from a config is marked `followsConfig`, so it reaches the policy even when it equals the preset default, and the format runs enforce it over a different value in the head config (Python flags, a ktlint section appended to every mirrored `.editorconfig`, a detekt `enforced` layer written to its own file). A width outside 10 to 999 is ignored with a printed note, as are `LineLength` modules with severity `ignore` and detekt rules with `active: false`. The TypeScript preset has no project-config convention, and `prettier.printWidth` drives no limit rule, so it has nothing to read from the base. For the kotlin preset the Java and Kotlin styles are merged in `definitions.mjs`. `javaLineLength(conventions)` feeds `javaLineLengthFindings`.
  - **Kotlin** (`languages/java/conventions/`): `kotlin.indentation` reuses the Java block-step measure and has no preset default, because ktfmt's is 2 and ktlint's is 4, so a choice always reaches the tools: ktfmt style `meta` or `kotlinlang`, ktlint `indent_size`; `kotlin.trailingCommas` compares wrapped call and declaration lists that end with a comma with those that do not (ktfmt always adds them, so only ktlint is affected); `kotlin.lineLength` (`measures/kotlin-line-length.mjs`, 100, 120 or 140, no preset default) is the `python.lineLength` rule for Kotlin: the longest breakable line against the shortest join of a wrapped `(...)` list, else lines past 100 or 120; ktlint gets `max_line_length` in its defaults file, ktfmt `--max-width` (`-Pnitpicker.kotlin.ktfmtMaxWidth`), detekt a `MaxLineLength` convention layer, and with no formatter it is unused; `kotlin.wildcardImports` marks a file `wildcard` when it has any wildcard import except the `java.util.*` and Android synthetics that detekt and ktlint allow anyway. `java.wildcardImports` (`measures/java-imports.mjs`; any star import except a static one in a test source set marks a file `wildcard`) and `java.testStaticImports` (static star imports, measured over the `src/*test*` source sets) are applied after the tools ran: `withoutFollowedImports` (`adapters/imports/`) drops `checkstyle/AvoidStarImport` and `errorprone/WildcardImport` findings before the overlap step, the latter only on `import static` lines of test files, so Gradle, Maven and the Kotlin preset's Java files behave alike; the detector passes the file path to `measure(text, file)`. `java.indentation` (Java sources, google-java-format 2 or `--aosp` 4) has the preset default 2, `kotlin.indentation` has none; both use the same measure. The ktlint and ktfmt runs happen in a mirror of the Kotlin sources (`nitpickerKotlinStyleWorkspace`) with a generated root `.editorconfig`, so project `.editorconfig` files stay out unless the tool lists the project; ktlint gets the conventions as `--editorconfig` defaults, detekt as a layer below the project's config (`nitpickerDetektLayers`).

## Parameter rules across presets

An unused parameter is one the body never reads; a boolean flag parameter is a boolean parameter of
an exported (public) function that the body branches on. The definitions agree across presets; what
differs is the language's own spelling, in particular the marker that says "this parameter is
required by a signature".

| Preset                 | Unused parameter                                                                 | Marker                  | Boolean flag parameter                                                                        |
| ---------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Python                 | ruff `ARG001`-`ARG005`; `@override`, `@abstractmethod`, dunders skipped          | a `_` prefix            | script `boolean-flag-parameter`: a positional pure `bool` used as a condition                 |
| TypeScript, JavaScript | `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` with `args: 'all'`, held to the edited lines | a `_` prefix            | `nitpicker/no-boolean-flag-parameter` (the guards branch)                                     |
| Kotlin                 | detekt `UnusedParameter`; overrides, `main` and annotated functions skipped      | `ignored*` or `unused*` | source check `BooleanFlagParameter`: a `Boolean` used as an `if`, `when` or `while` condition |
| Java                   | PMD `UnusedFormalParameter`; overrides and annotated methods skipped             | `ignored*` or `unused*` | `BooleanFlagParameter` (the guards branch)                                                    |

Python, TypeScript and JavaScript take the `_` prefix because the language and its linters treat it
as the discard marker. Kotlin parameters cannot be named `_`, and PMD's convention is the `ignored`
and `unused` prefixes, so Java and Kotlin share those. A boolean flag parameter is fixed the same
way everywhere: a keyword-only parameter (Python), two functions named for each behavior, or an
enum or options object. Overrides, interface and abstract methods, setters, builders and factories,
private and nested functions, and tests are skipped; a flag that is only passed on or stored is not
a flag.

## Dependency and repository hygiene (`shared/hygiene/`)

`repository-hygiene` is added to every preset's analyzers by `core/configuration/preset-analyzers.mjs` (injected analyzers still replace the whole set). It runs `core/cli.mjs hygiene --check repository-hygiene --project-root <root>` (the CLI entry hands the arguments to `runHygieneCli` in `runner.mjs`, so the runner needs no entry point of its own; the rule ids and the catalog rows live in `shared/project-rules/hygiene-rules.mjs`, which `shared/policy.mjs` imports), lists files with `git ls-files -z` and reads the head policy file for `exclude` and `exceptions`: an excepted finding is dropped inside the runner, by rule id and project-relative path.

- **Identities.** `file:<path>`, so a file already on the base stays unchanged and only an added one is new.
- **Sizes.** `git ls-files -s` and `git cat-file --batch-check` size the committed blob of each regular file, so an LFS pointer is small and a symlink or submodule is not sized.
- **Configuration hash.** The runner's relative-import closure (`shared/import-closure.mjs`); the TypeScript analyzers hash the import closure of their adapter files, which reaches the dependency ESLint rule and `quality-areas/reporting/dead-code.mjs`.
- **Key blocks.** `git grep -l -I -E` finds the private-key header in any tracked text file.
- **Package rules.** `languages/typescript/adapters/quality-areas/reporting/dead-code.mjs` maps knip's `dependencies` and `devDependencies` to `unused-dependency`, `unlisted` to `unlisted-dependency` and `unresolved` to `unresolved-import`, keyed by package name or specifier. Their exceptions come from the head policy file like the rules above; an entry with `packages` lifts only findings whose package name or specifier it lists. `knip.json` lists `ignoreDependencies` for the packages nitpicker itself needs. `nitpicker/no-dev-dependency-in-production` (in `eslint-lint`) reads the nearest `package.json` and flags value imports of a package that is only in `devDependencies`.

## Change history (`shared/history/`)

`change-history` is an analyzer of its own that reuses the hygiene plumbing: it runs `core/cli.mjs history --check change-history --project-root <root> [--history-ref <sha>]` (`runHistoryCli` in `shared/history/runner.mjs`, which shares `runCheckCli` and the argument parser of `shared/hygiene/cli.mjs`, reads the head policy file and drops excepted findings by rule id and module path like the hygiene runner). `createHistoryAnalyzers` (`shared/history/analyzers.mjs`) builds it with `createCheckAnalyzer`, whose configuration hash is the import closure of `history/runner.mjs`, so the hygiene hash does not move when history code changes. Its `resolveConfigurationHash(policyRoot)` adds `cloneDepth` (`full` or `shallow` from `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` in the head snapshot, or the unavailable reason when git cannot tell) to that hash, so a base result cached by a shallow run (always unavailable, so empty) is never reused by a full-history run on the same merge base. The git calls share the `git` wrapper of `shared/hygiene/tracked-files.mjs`, which pipes stderr so a failure carries git's own message. `core/configuration/preset-analyzers.mjs` adds it to any preset's analyzers only when `config.history.coChange` is `true` (injected analyzers still replace the whole set; `disable` names it like any analyzer, and `"disable": ["change-history"]` without the opt-in is an unknown-analyzer error). The check lists the files of the history ref (`committedFiles`, `git ls-tree -r` at the ref) only once the history is available, so a project that is not in a repository is "unavailable", not a tool error. The rule id `co-change`, its catalog row, the defaults and the limits (`CO_CHANGE_DEFAULTS`, `CO_CHANGE_LIMITS`) are in `shared/project-rules/hygiene-rules.mjs`; `enforcedBy` is `true` for every language with `reportOnly: true`, which `shared/checks-catalog.mjs` prints as `report-only` like the design rules, and the console summary lists its new findings with the design notes.

- **Config.** `history` is `{ coChange, commits, minSupport, minConfidence, maxCommitFiles }`, validated by `core/configuration/scopes/history-settings.mjs` (a setting without `coChange` is an error; with `coChange: false` it is validated and kept, so `false` is a clean off switch; `maxCommitFiles` is at most `CO_CHANGE_LIMITS.maxCommitFiles` = 100). It is a head-policy key (`shared/head-policy.mjs`), so it is part of the cache key. It is not a loosening in `core/policy-changes/`: turning it on or off, or moving a threshold, is listed as a plain policy change, because the findings never fail. The base is measured with the head's `history` (`baseConfigCoveringHead`, as for `duplicates`), otherwise a base without the key would report no pairs and every pair would be new. `layout.featureRoots` is accepted by every preset when `coChange` is on (`validateFactRuleSettings`, `validateLayout`).
- **History.** `evidence/git-history.mjs` runs `git log -n <commits> --no-merges -M --name-status -z --relative --format=%x01%H --end-of-options <ref> -- .` in the project root (so a project below the repository root sees only its own paths) after `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` and an empty-repository probe (`git rev-list -n 1 --all`). It returns `{ available: false, reason }` for `shallow`, `not-a-repository`, `too-few` (fewer than `CO_CHANGE_LIMITS.minHistory` = 20 commits, 0 in an empty repository), `git-missing` (the spawn failed with `ENOENT`) and `unreadable` (with the first line of git's stderr as `detail`), never an exception. `<ref>` is `HEAD` unless `--history-ref` names one. Both sides of a comparison read the same window: the base worktree is checked out at the merge base, and `createInvocation` of the head run adds `--history-ref` with `git rev-parse HEAD` of `baselineRoot` (the base worktree), so the head reads the merge base's window too; commits the change adds, the synthetic commit of a dirty tree and a window that would shift by those commits never reach either side. Modules are resolved against the files of the ref, not the head's tracked files.
- **Evidence.** `evidence/evidence.mjs` walks commits newest first. Every rename entry maps its old path to the current path of the new one, so older commits count for the file where it lives now (also for commits that are skipped). A commit counts when it has at most `maxCommitFiles` entries (all files, tests and documents included) and edits (`M`, `T`, or a rename that is not `R100`) at least one production file; its modules are the modules of its edited production files only, so added files, pure renames and edits to tests and documents are no evidence, and a pair needs edits in both modules. Modules get integer ids and a pair key is `first * 2^21 + second`; pairs are counted only between modules with at least `minSupport` commits, which keeps the pair map small; the cost per commit is quadratic in its modules, bounded by the 100-file cap. `confidence` is `support / min(commits(a), commits(b))`; a pair is reported at `support >= minSupport` and `confidence >= minConfidence`, unless one module is the parent folder of the other. Sample size is the number of counting commits.
- **Modules.** `evidence/production-files.mjs` keeps a file of the ref that has a code extension (ts, tsx, mts, cts, js, jsx, mjs, cjs, py, java, kt, kts), is not a declaration file (`.d.ts`, `.d.mts`, `.d.cts`), a build or tool file (`*.gradle`, `*.gradle.kts`, `*.config.*`, `.*rc.js`, `setup.py`, `conftest.py`), a test (`layout.testRoots`, a test name, a test folder at any depth, a test source set under `src/`, or a root `fixtures/` or `testing/`; under `src/main/` only the name counts), generated (output folders such as `build/` and `out/` at any depth, generated names, `generatedFiles` of the head policy, a generator header or `@Generated` annotation via `shared/conventions/generator-marker.mjs`; a file missing from the working tree is not treated as generated) and is not under `exclude`. Its module is `featureRoles` (`shared/rules/features/feature-roles.mjs`, the function `shared-used-by-one-feature` uses, with no shared roots) feature folder, else the folder holding it; files in the project root have no module.
- **Findings.** `tool` `change-history`, `rule` `co-change`, `path` the smaller module (plain string order), `identity` `<a>|<b>`, `classification` `descriptive`, `exceptionPaths` both modules with and without a trailing `/` (stripped by the runner after the `exceptions` check, so an entry for either module lifts it). The message has three parts: the evidence (support, sample size, confidence over the less-changed module's commits, up to `CO_CHANGE_EXAMPLES` short commit ids), one remedy sentence that says co-change is correlation, and the exception route with a `<module>/**` glob. The longer reading (which way out fits, why nitpicker does not read imports at this layer) is in `docs/usage.md#change-history`.
