The report directory root also holds `duplicates.json` and `kotlin-duplicates.json`, written by the Node side.
- **Structure metrics.** `StructureMetrics.java` runs as `java StructureMetrics.java <structure.json> <classes.json> <analysisRoot> <sourceRoot>...` in the JDK's single-file source mode (task `nitpickerStructure`, no dependency beyond the JDK that runs Gradle). It parses, never compiles, the main sources with the compiler tree API and writes one object per method, constructor, static or instance initializer block with a statement (`Owner.<clinit>`, `Owner.<init>`, `initializer: true`), field initializer that branches (`Owner.field`, `initializer: true`) and lambda (`Owner.method.<lambda>`, or `Owner.method.local` / `Owner.field` when it initializes a variable or field, `lambda: true`); members of nested, anonymous and local classes are their own entries, and no unit counts towards the one around it (a lambda's statements, nesting and complexity are its own; `lines` is the physical span). Fields: `lines` (last line - first line + 1, annotations and modifiers included, Javadoc not), `statements` (every statement below the body except blocks), `maxControlNesting` (deepest nesting of `if`, `for`, enhanced `for`, `while`, `do`, `switch` and `catch`; an `else if` stays on the level of the `if` it continues), `parameters`, `complexity` (1 + `if`, `for`, enhanced `for`, `while`, `do`, `catch`, `?:`, `&&`, `||` and each non-default `case`, an arrow case or a case with several constants once; a switch expression or pattern switch whose arrow cases each hold one expression or a `throw` counts once plus once per `when` guard, never more than its cases), `signature` (`Owner.method(parameter types)`, the CRAP identity; the unit name for an initializer or a lambda), `parameterTypes` (without generics or package, arrays and varargs as `Name[]`, a type variable of the method or an enclosing class as its first bound, `Object` without one), `privateEmptyConstructor`, `initializer` and `lambda`. A syntax error prints `file:line` and the compiler message and exits with status 1, so the task fails instead of dropping methods.
- **Fake Gradle for tests.** `adapters/fixtures/fake-gradle.mjs` stands in for Gradle in tests that run without Java (`NITPICKER_GRADLE`). In the project directory: `GRADLE_FAIL` fails the run with its text; `NO_REPORTS` writes an empty `modules.json`; each line of `violations.txt` is a PMD violation in `src/main/java/demo/A.java`; `jacoco.xml` and `mutations.xml` become those reports; `modules.txt` lists more projects, one directory per line, each with the same reports under its folder and key `module-<directory>`; a project folder's `jdeps.txt` and `jdeps-classes.txt` are copied into its reports. With `-Pnitpicker.kotlin=true`, `detekt.txt` (`rule :: message` or a message for `detekt.style.MagicNumber`), `ktlint.txt` (`rule|message`), `kotlin-format.txt` and `kotlin-metrics.json` feed the Kotlin reports. Reports go to `-Pnitpicker.reportDir`; the arguments are kept in `build/gradle-args.json`. Tests call it in process: `runGradle` (`adapters/gradle-wrapper/gradle-process.mjs`) takes the function that starts its supervisor as a `spawn` option (`spawnSync` by default), and `spawnFakeGradle` in `adapters/test-support/in-process-gradle.mjs` answers the supervisor call for the fake's path with `runFakeGradle` (`adapters/test-support/fake-gradle/`; the fixture is the program around it).
- **Fake Maven for tests.** `core/command/java/test-support/` holds `fake-maven.mjs` and `fake-jdk.mjs`, two pure functions, `runFakeMaven({ args, cwd })` and `runFakeJdk({ tool, args, cwd })`, which `spawnFakeMaven` in `in-process-maven.mjs` answers in process. The Maven preset takes an injected `spawn` (`runMavenPreset`, `runMaven`, `runJava`, `writeJdepsReports` and the direct `jar` and `java -version` calls; `spawnSync` by default), `runTasksWithFakeMaven` in `java-cli-harness.mjs` passes the fake, `NITPICKER_MAVEN` is the path of `fake-maven.mjs`, a program name of `java`, `jdeps` or `jar` is the fake JDK wherever it is found (a non-fake command goes to the real `spawnSync`), and `useMavenFixtures` points `XDG_CACHE_HOME` at an empty directory, so no process starts and the real Maven preset (effective POM, build, tool resolution, every tool outside Maven) runs without Java tools or network. The fake Maven copies `pom.xml` as the effective POM, writes one `classpath.txt` per tool of the tools project, and for the build creates `target/classes` and `target/test-classes` (with a class per source file when `MAIN_CLASSES` exists) and the dependency list, copies `test-results/test/*.xml` to `target/surefire-reports/` with a JaCoCo execution file; `MAVEN_FAIL` makes the build fail with `[ERROR] <its text>`. The fake JDK writes the reports the Gradle fake writes (`violations.txt` as PMD violations, `jacoco.xml`, `mutations.xml`, `jdeps/jdeps.txt` and `jdeps/jdeps-test.txt` as the `jdeps` output, empty SpotBugs, Checkstyle, structure and Error Prone reports). `java-cli.test.mjs` and `java-cli-legacy-inventory.test.mjs` run every case against both fakes.
- **Project settings the init script overrides.** After evaluation it resets the JaCoCo filters of every `Test` task (`jacoco` includes, excludes and class-loader excludes; `jacocoTestReport.classDirectories` back to the main output) and replaces the Checkstyle config of every Checkstyle task. `pitest-junit5-plugin` and a launcher are on PIT's classpath only when the test runtime has `junit-platform-engine`, `pitest-testng-plugin` only when it has a `testng` jar. `nitpickerArchunit` takes JUnit 4 for a declared `archunit-junit4`, the JUnit Platform for `archunit-junit5`, and the framework of the project's `test` task with both or neither; it fails when ArchUnit classes exist and none produced a report. PMD's `TestClassWithoutTestCases` runs from a generated `pmd-tests.xml` that excludes the main source directories, as the main-only rules (`pmd-main-ruleset.xml`, and the Spring rules) run from `pmd-main.xml` that excludes the test ones.
- **`ClassLoadEffect` XPath.** The rule in `pmd-main-ruleset.xml` is one expression: top-level `let` lists of method names (`$calls`, `$fileCalls`, `$restCalls`, `$reactiveCalls`) and `$filesImported` (a static import from `Files`), then one `//MethodCall` pass whose first predicate keeps only calls with a listed name, a `new*` name, a type receiver or, with `$filesImported`, no receiver, before any `matchesSig` or `typeIs` runs, and one `//ConstructorCall` pass filtered by the written simple name before `typeIs`. One trailing predicate holds the load-time test as inline functions: `$owner` (the nearest member or lambda), `$typeOf`, `$isStatic`, `$candidates` (the canonical names a parameter type may resolve to: single import, a type of the file, else star imports, `java.lang` and the package), `$resolvesTo` (a call matches a declaration when `matchesSig` accepts its shape, primitives by kind and references as `_`, and, per reference parameter, one of its candidate names; varargs as `_*`), `$invokes`, `$creators` (static members and the static methods they call), `$constructorAtLoad` (created by a creator, or reached through one `this(...)`), `$instancesAtLoad` (an anonymous class in a static member or enum constant, or a `new T(...)` in a creator), `$direct` and `$helper` (a method of the same type that a non-method direct member invokes). Inside these functions there is no context item, so the compilation unit is `root($node)`. Merging the effect tests into the two passes instead of one predicate per node halves the rule's time on a large tree.
- **Gradle failure text.** `condenseFailure` (`adapters/gradle-wrapper/diagnostics/failure-text.mjs`) drops the JVM's `Picked up JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS` notices, PIT's `INFO` progress lines, `[ant:checkstyle]` warnings and `SLF4J(W):` no-provider warnings (the Checkstyle tasks also run with `showViolations = false`). When the text is still too long it keeps Gradle's failure block (from `FAILURE:` or `* What went wrong:`, without the `* Try:` hints) whole, every distinct `Caused by:` line before it, and as much of the start as fits, cutting the middle; without a failure block it keeps the start and the end.

### Ingesters

Ingesters turn tool reports into findings:

- **Maven builds.** `detectJavaBuild` (`languages/java/builds/java-build.mjs`) picks the build; `languages/java/maven/` writes the same reports per module in `<module>/target/nitpicker` and `readModuleFindings` (`builds/java-modules.mjs`) prefixes each module's findings. The tool versions on both sides come from the init script's `def pinned = [...]` map, parsed by `readPinnedTools` with a regex, so keep that map one `name: 'version'` pair per entry. The build runs `test`, or `verify` when the effective POM of any module binds maven-failsafe-plugin's `integration-test` goal in an execution that the project does not keep off (`skipITs`/`skipTests` properties, `skip`/`skipITs`/`skipTests` configuration, phase `none`): `runsFailsafe` from `describeModule`, arguments from `lifecycleArgs` in `maven-project.mjs`, which adds `-Djacoco.haltOnFailure=false` and the skip properties of signing, publishing, network and release-check plugins (listed in java-preset.md). Failing Failsafe tests are tool errors like failing Surefire tests, and their reports are copied to `test-results/failsafe/`. On the base snapshot `collectTestResults` (`maven-tests.mjs`) lists the failing Surefire and Failsafe cases instead of throwing: `analyzeModule` then skips JaCoCo and PIT for that module, its manifest says `testsFailed: true` (the Gradle manifest does too), and `runMavenPreset` returns the joined detail as `testsFailure`, which `collectJavaFindings` turns into the `tests-failed` tool error that the core reads as a red base. Surefire and Failsafe reports and coverage files count as fresh when modified at most 2 s before the build started (file systems with coarse timestamps); our JaCoCo file is `target/nitpicker-jacoco.exec`, which the Failsafe JVM appends to through the same `argLine`, and every fresh file among it and the `destFile`s of the project's `prepare-agent` and `prepare-agent-integration` executions goes to one JaCoCo report. Maven runs at most 15 minutes; a timeout is a `java/maven-failure` that names the limit. The resolved tool classpaths are cached per user in `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/nitpicker` (else `~/.cache/nitpicker`), keyed by a hash of the tool coordinates; base and head run one after the other, so they never resolve at the same time.
- **XML text.** `parseXml` sets `text` on a leaf element (entities decoded, comments dropped, CDATA kept verbatim); an empty leaf has no `text`.

- **Common.** Repeats of one identity within a report get `#n` in report order. The XML parser reads elements and attributes only and throws `TypeError` when malformed.
- **SARIF** (PMD, SpotBugs). Identity is `partialFingerprints` (sorted) when present, else a hash of rule, path and symbol. Absolute `file://` URIs (PMD) and source-root-relative paths (SpotBugs) are made project-relative; results outside the project are skipped. SpotBugs `note`-level results are promoted because the tool already runs at the configured threshold, and its subject comes from message arguments since the text is generic. PMD's `ClassLoadEffect` results pass through `withLoadEffectSubjects` (`messages/load-effects.mjs`) first: it reads the source file once per report, blanks literals and comments, and `javaOutline` (`messages/java-members.mjs`) lists each type with its members (field, static block, instance initializer, constructor, method, enum constant, nested type) and their offsets; `placeAt` finds the member at the result's column, climbing out of anonymous classes to the named type's member. The rule's generic place sentence is replaced by the call (`shortCall`: arguments and lambdas collapsed, a chain split over lines joined) and the member, and `partialFingerprints['nitpicker/subject']` is set to `Type.member: call`, so the identity has no line number. For a finding in a method, the first field, static block or enum constant whose text calls the method by name (else a constructor or initializer) is named as the site.
- **Checkstyle.** Identity hashes the check `source`, path and message; the line is location only.
- **ArchUnit.** One finding per violated rule and offending class, identified by the fully qualified class; line numbers and violation counts never take part. The path is resolved by `options.resolvePath(className, fileName)`, falling back to the declaring test class. A failed case without violation lines yields one `test-failure`.
- **Spring architecture rules.** `languages/java/gradle/archunit/` holds `ArchRules.java` and helpers (in the default package, in subfolders too: every `.java` file under it is compiled, sorted by path), compiled by the `nitpickerSpringArch` task with `javac` from the JDK that runs Gradle against the pinned ArchUnit and ASM (`asm-analysis`, which brings `asm-tree` and `asm`) jars into `<Gradle user home>/caches/nitpicker/spring-arch/<sha256 of the rule sources, the ArchUnit and ASM versions and the JDK vendor and version>` (compiled into a uniquely named sibling, then moved there atomically; a concurrent run that loses the race drops its copy), then run as `java @run.args [-Dnitpicker.spring.roles=<file>] ArchRules <output.json> <main-class-dirs> <unit-test-class-dirs> [<other-test-class-dirs>]` (class directories joined by the path separator; the unit ones are the `test` source set's, the other ones every other test source set's, and Maven passes its one test output directory as the unit one and no other; the argument file carries the classpath: ArchUnit, the compiled rules, both compile classpaths and the class directories) in `build/nitpicker/spring-arch/`. The task runs only when `runsArchRules()`: `usesSpring()`, or a `jmolecules-ddd-*.jar` on the main compile classpath (Maven: `runsArchRules` in `languages/java/maven/spring/arch-rules-gate.mjs`, the same jar test). Output `spring-arch.json`: `{ "root": "<root package or null>", "violations": [{ "rule", "className", "fileName", "detail" }] }`, sorted and deduplicated by rule, class and detail. `className` is the outermost class's binary name, `fileName` its compiled-from source file (empty when unknown), `detail` the dependency's class name, `<method>:<entity>` for `spring-controller-entity`, `<scope>:<injected type>` for `spring-scope-capture` on a component and `<method>:<scope>:<injected type>` on a `@Bean` method (the scope is the alphabetically first of the matching beans), `<aggregate root>:<entity>` for `ddd-foreign-entity`, `<field>:<operation>:<source>` for `spring-request-state` (a static field as `<Class>.<field>`, the operation `=`, `[]` or the mutator or project method name, the source `<Class>.<method>(<parameter>)` or `<Holder>.<method>()`), the layer package for `spring-layout`, or empty. The reader drops a `spring-feature-boundary` entry when a layering rule (`spring-controller-repository`, `spring-controller-controller`, `spring-repository-service`, `spring-service-web`) has the same class and detail and no exception lifts that layering finding (`isExcepted`, which `java-reports.mjs` builds from the config's exceptions with the module path), so an excepted layering finding never hides the boundary finding. The roles file (`nitpicker.spring.roles`, written from the config's `spring.roles` by `spring-arch/spring-roles.mjs` into the analysis directory and passed as `-Pnitpicker.spring.roles=<file>` on Gradle or straight to the JVM on Maven) has one `<role>=<binary name>` line per mapping; `Roles.java` gives a class a role when it is meta-annotated with, or (being another type) assignable to, a mapped name, on top of the stereotypes. `spring-arch/spring-arch.mjs` (`readSpringArchFindings`) turns each entry into an enforceable `archunit` finding: symbol the simple class name, identity that name (empty detail) or `<name>|<short detail>`, where the short detail drops the package of each `:`-separated part (`all:Customer`, `pricing:request:Basket`, `HttpServletRequest`) so a package move matched as a rename keeps the identity (for `spring-request-state` the short detail is the field alone, so another handler reaching the same field keeps it), with `#n` for repeats counted per resolved file, path from `resolvePath(className, fileName)` (an unresolvable class is skipped, an unknown rule or a missing file throws), message from a per-rule template that leads with the allowed form plus `exceptionHint` (`shared/rules/exceptions.mjs`). `ArchRules` runs the layout, layering, lifetime and test rules only when `org.springframework.context.ApplicationContext` loads from its classpath (the project's compile classpaths), so a jMolecules-only module gets the aggregate rules alone. `ddd/DddModel.java` builds the membership (roots, typed entities with their owner from the generic binding, `@Entity` classes owned by the roots that reach them, value objects; abstract classes and interfaces included) and `ddd/HeldTypes.java` walks a class's own fields and those of superclasses that are not members themselves, resolving type variables through the subclass's bindings; roots compare by assignability, so a subtype or an interface root counts as the same aggregate. `state/` holds `spring-request-state`: `flow/Flows.java` reads each class file with ASM (`asm-analysis`, pinned as `asm` next to ArchUnit in the init script and the Maven tool list, and part of the rule cache hash) and runs `flow/FlowInterpreter.java` over every method on demand (memoized, at most 128 nested analyses deep), keeping taint and alias sets per value (`flow/Aliases.java`: `field:` for the receiver's fields, `held:<class>#<field>` for a field of another shared object or of the outer class of an inner class, `static:`, `new:`, `arg:`), with getter results, returned request sources and Kotlin coroutine spill fields resolved per callee; `Summaries.java` settles per-argument summaries (which fields an argument reaches, which arguments retain it, which field writes depend on the receiver so a locally created bean instance does not count) with a worklist over the project's call graph (each method recomputed at most 64 times), and `RequestState.java` seeds the handler parameters and request sources and reports each reached shared or static field once. `ArchRules` reports every context-starting class of the unit test directories under `spring-test-placement`, and the reader drops those that `isIntegrationTest` (`adapters/test-times/integration-tests.mjs`, also used by the slow-test budget) accepts by name or package, so the two checks share one definition of an integration test. The init script writes `springArch: true` to `manifest.json` whenever the task runs (`runsArchRules()`), and `java-reports.mjs` reads the report only when the manifest says so; the Maven build writes `springArch: <spring or jmolecules-ddd>` per module too (`springFacts` and `withDeclaredSpring` in `spring/arch-rules-gate.mjs`, which also resolves ArchUnit for a build whose `pom.xml` declares Spring), and `languages/java/maven/spring/maven-spring-arch.mjs` compiles the same rule sources with the JDK's `javac` against the ArchUnit and ASM jars (resolved with the other Maven tools only when some module runs the rules) into `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/nitpicker/spring-arch/<same hash>` (else `~/.cache/nitpicker/spring-arch/<hash>`, a separate cache from Gradle's), and runs `ArchRules` with `<module>/target/nitpicker/spring-arch.json` as output.
- **CRAP.** `crap = ccn^2 * (1 - coverage)^3 + ccn`, with coverage the lower of line ("statement") and branch coverage. `crap-score-limit` when over `options.limit`; `crap-coverage-unknown` when no JaCoCo method matches. Identity is the method signature (`Calc.classify(int, int, String)`, from `structure.json`) plus `#n`; for the `<init>`, `<clinit>` and `<lambda>` units `#n` counts only the entries of the same name and source over the limit (`repeatsInSource` in `shared/rules/finding-helpers.mjs`, also used by `structureFindings`). Java's complexity is `complexity` of `structure.json` (`StructureMetrics.java`), and a `privateEmptyConstructor` entry is left out (JaCoCo often omits those, and their complexity of 1 cannot exceed the limit). A JaCoCo method matches a function when file and name agree (`<init>` is the class name) and its first line lies in the function's range, or, for a constructor, when the descriptor's parameter types match `parameterTypes` (aligned at the end, so synthetic leading parameters of inner classes and enums are skipped; `StructureMetrics.java` writes a type variable as the erasure of its first bound, as the descriptor has it). The JaCoCo `<init>` of a class with field initializers starts at the first initializer, before the constructor. A function at 100% coverage gets `crapScoreLimitCovered`, Java's unknown coverage `crapCoverageUnknownJava`. `options.sourceRoots` maps the project-relative paths to JaCoCo's package-relative ones. `ingestCrapFunctions` is the same ingester over already parsed functions; its `selectMethods` option replaces the name-and-range match (the Kotlin preset passes `selectKotlinMethods`).
- **Format check.** One `format` finding per file in the list; identity is the constant `format`. `javaStyle()` turns the `java.formatter` and `java.indentation` conventions into `google`, `aosp`, `palantir` or `none`: the Gradle and Maven runs add `--aosp` to google-java-format for `aosp`, run palantir-java-format (`com.palantir.javaformat.java.Main --palantir`, pinned as `palantirJavaFormat`, a `format-palantir` classpath in the Maven tool project) for `palantir`, and write an empty `format.txt` for `none` (the Maven tool project then resolves no formatter jar: `formatTool` in `maven-tools.mjs` adds `format` or `format-palantir` as an extra tool), and the finding's tool is `google-java-format` or `palantir-java-format`; its message gives the fix command and the `conventions.java.formatter` pin (`JAVA_FORMATTER_TOOLS` in `java-style.mjs`, also used by `layoutFix` in `rule-messages.mjs` for the `layout` Checkstyle findings, which say to fix by hand under `none`). The runner reads the convention from the head policy file (`NITPICKER_POLICY`) when it starts, so it is not in the analyzer's hash, and passes the style as `-Pnitpicker.java.style=aosp` to Gradle (plus `-Pnitpicker.java.indentation` under `none`) and as `javaStyle` to `runFormat` in `maven-lint.mjs`. Checkstyle's layout checks (`Indentation` among them) are suppressed for every formatter style by `checkstyle-suppressions.xml`, which the Maven run's `checkstyleSetup` passes too, so only the formatter reports layout. Under `none` both runs use `checkstyle-suppressions-unformatted.xml` instead, which keeps the whitespace, brace, tab and indentation checks, and add `UnusedImports` to the bundled config (`unformatted-checks.mjs` for Maven, the same edit in `nitpicker.init.gradle`; `Indentation` moves to the measured width, and tab-indented files drop it and `FileTabCharacter`), so unused imports, which Error Prone's off `RemoveUnusedImports` and the formatter no longer cover, are still findings.
- **Class limits.** `StructureMetrics.java` writes `classes.json` in the same parse as `structure.json`: `{ files: { <path>: { package, imports[] } }, classes: [{ file, name, start, kind, methods, fields, extends? }] }`. `name` is `Outer.Inner`, `kind` is `class`, `interface`, `enum`, `record` or `annotation`, `extends` the superclass as written without type arguments. `methods` are declared methods (constructors not), `fields` instance fields and record components. `inheritanceDepths` resolves `extends` by enclosing names, imports, the same package, wildcard imports and the name in full; a superclass it cannot find counts as one level. One finding per class and limit (`class-methods-limit`, `class-fields-limit`, `inheritance-depth-limit`), identity `<metric>:<name>:occurrence:<n>:<value>`, ratchet key the same without the value. `shared/project-rules/size-rules.mjs` holds the size rule ids, defaults, the Java line length and the message templates, and `shared/policy.mjs` merges them into its tables. `StructureMetrics.java` is run as `<structure.json> <classes.json> <analysisRoot> <sourceRoot>...` and writes both reports from one parse, by the Gradle `nitpickerStructure` task and by `runStructure` in `maven-build.mjs` for each Maven module. `readModuleFindings` in `java-reports.mjs` reads `classes.json` per module, for both presets (the Kotlin preset's `.java` files keep the class limits), with `modulePath` so an exception path and the one a message prints are project-relative in a multi-module Maven build. A missing `classes.json` throws, so the run reports a tool error naming the structure step instead of dropping the limits. The folder-depth root package is the common package prefix of the base snapshot's code (`policy.baselineRoot` on a head run, the snapshot itself on a base run), so a branch cannot move it.
- **Folder depth.** `folder-depth-limit` is one finding per folder and kind of file (`folder-depth:<kind>:<depth>`), `ratchet.pathIsFolder` with `ratchet.folderFiles` (the files in the folder, since `value` is the depth) so a moved folder is matched when more than half its files were renamed to one place. Java: levels below the root package, the longest package prefix the base snapshot's code shares (`module-info.java`, `package-info.java` and default-package files do not vote). TypeScript: folders below `features/<feature>/<layer>/` under a source or test root.
- **Coverage.** Go coverprofile gives a `statement-coverage` metric per file only (blocks lack function names, and line-based identity is not allowed); `options.modulePath` is stripped from import paths. JaCoCo gives `line-coverage` plus `uncovered-method` (class and method name+descriptor); `options.sourceRoot` is prepended. lcov gives `line-coverage` plus `uncovered-function`.
- **PIT.** Follows `docs/mutation-testing.md`: no score gate, new surviving or uncovered mutants fail, other non-killed statuses are report-only, counts go to `mutation-summary`. A mutant is identified by simple class name, method with its descriptor, mutator and description plus its occurrence `#n` among identical keys in the same source file (by line, then block index), so a same-named class in another package has its own count; package names are dropped from the class, the descriptor's `L...;` types and the description's `a/b/C` names, so a package move matched as a git rename keeps the identity. Mutants with unresolvable sources (generated code) are skipped.
- **Duplicates.** jscpd runs once per build, per existing main source root of the merged manifest, writing `duplicates.json` in the report directory as `{ runs: [{ root, report }] }`. Findings match the TypeScript ones: `duplicate-clone` and `duplicate-clone-size`, identified by file pair, cloned-text hash and occurrence.
- **Fan-out bounds.** `UnboundedFanOut` is one XPath rule in `gradle/pmd-main-ruleset.xml`, so Gradle and Maven run the same text. It reports the per-element site (the stream call, or the loop) rather than the task start, so one finding stands for one fan-out however many tasks the body starts; a task start counts for the nearest enclosing site only, so nested loops report the inner one. Helpers are followed by name and arity in the enclosing class body, three levels deep, by layering one non-recursive XPath function over the previous one. The message is static in PMD, and `messages/fan-out/fan-out-details.mjs` (called from `sarifFindings`) replaces its lead with the collection and the task start read from the source at the reported line; when nothing matches the generic lead stays. The identity is the static message, numbered per file. A cut (`subList`, `limit`) is a bound only when its size arguments read no `size()` or `length` (directly or through a local initializer), `Math.min` with one size-free argument included; a range is fixed on the same test, so a worker pool sized by a count passes. `partition`, `chunked` and `batches` bound only the chunk variable that iterates them, since a task per batch still grows with the collection.

## Kotlin preset (`languages/java/`)

The Kotlin preset is the Java preset with `kotlin: true`: same init script, runner and ingesters. User-facing behavior is in `docs/kotlin-preset.md`; the protocol details:

- **Switch.** `gradle -Pnitpicker.kotlin=true` turns the Kotlin tasks on in modules applying `org.jetbrains.kotlin.jvm`; without it the init script is the Java one. The runner takes `--preset kotlin`, `--kotlin-formatter` and `--kotlin-detekt-config` (from `kotlin.formatter` and `kotlin.detektConfig`). Gradle always runs with `--no-configuration-cache` (both presets): the init script registers tasks and reads project state at execution time, which a project's `org.gradle.configuration-cache=true` would fail on.
- **Properties.** `kotlin-tools.mjs` resolves, per snapshot, what the init script needs and passes it as `-Pnitpicker.kotlin.formatter=<ktlint|ktfmt|none>`, `.ktfmtStyle=<kotlinlang|google|meta>`, `.ktfmtEditorconfig=true` (ktfmt lists the project), `.styleFile=<path of a JSON file>` (written to a temporary folder by `kotlin-run.mjs` and removed after the run; `{ offset, editorconfigs: [{ path, text }], ktlintDefaults, detektConventions }`, generated by `kotlin-style-files.mjs` and `style/editorconfig-mirror.mjs`: the `.editorconfig` files under the policy root and above it up to the repository root, `offset` being the policy root inside the repository, so the init script mirrors the sources at `<offset>/<module>/...`), `.detektLayers=<conventions,project>` and `.detektConfig=<absolute path in the head checkout>`; the project style files are read from the policy root (the head checkout) for both snapshots. Without the formatter property the init script runs no format check. The analyzer is named `kotlin`, and its tool-error and `baseline-expansion` findings use that name.
- **Reports.** `detekt.sarif`, `ktlint.sarif` (ktlint's SARIF reporter, relative paths) and `ktfmt.txt` (project-relative Kotlin files, one per line); only the detected formatter's file is read. Skipped Java tools in a Kotlin-only module write empty reports so the Nitpicker side reads one layout. `manifest.json` in this mode: `sourceRoots` are the Java roots only, `kotlinSourceRoots` the Kotlin plugin's source directories (which include `src/main/java`), `kotlin: true`, `kotlinFormatter`. `readKotlinFindings` throws when `kotlin` is absent, so a project without the Kotlin plugin is a tool error and not an empty pass.
- **Java-only class tools.** `analyzedClassDirs` is the output of `compileJava` in this mode (all main class directories otherwise); SpotBugs reads it. SpotBugs gets the rest of the runtime classpath as `-auxclasspath`. PIT reads `mutatedClassDirs` (all main class directories, Java and Kotlin) in both presets, and jdeps reads every main class directory in both modes.
- **Function units.** Every preset measures the same units, so a limit means the same everywhere: a function, method or constructor with a body, a lambda, arrow function or anonymous function (TypeScript's callbacks, Java and Kotlin lambdas, Python `lambda`), and the code that runs at class initialization (Java static and instance initializer blocks, Kotlin `init` blocks and property initializers that branch). A unit's statements, control nesting and complexity never count towards the unit around it (TypeScript's analyzer stops at nested functions, and so do `StructureMetrics.java`, `kotlin-psi` and `structure_metrics.py`); `lines` is the physical span, so the lines of a nested unit still count towards the one around it, in every language. A lambda is named after the unit that holds it plus `<lambda>`, or after the variable or property it initializes; `repeatsInSource` makes `<init>`, `<clinit>` and `<lambda>` findings number only among the same name and source over the limit. Coverage: JaCoCo's `lambda$...` synthetic methods for Java, the method or the lines of the lambda for Kotlin, the line of the creating statement for Python (coverage.py has nothing finer). TypeScript's CRAP check (`@barney-media/crap-typescript-core`) scores named and assigned functions only and drops the complexity of an anonymous callback from the function around it, a limit of that library.
- **Kotlin metrics (KT3).** `languages/java/gradle/kotlin-psi/` is compiled with the `javac` of the JVM that runs Gradle (`kotlinPsiClasses` in the init script: once per build into `<root project build>/tmp/nitpickerKotlinPsi/<key>`, the key a hash of the program sources, the pinned compiler, its classpath and the Gradle JVM version, so a later build reuses it; a lock keeps modules that run in parallel to one compile, and a new key deletes the directories of keys this build did not use; a Gradle JVM without `javac` fails the task with a message to run on a JDK) and run by task `nitpickerKotlinPsi` as `java -cp <kotlin-compiler-embeddable and its dependencies>:<classes> nitpicker.kotlinpsi.KotlinPsi <metrics.json> <facts.json> <projectDir> <root>...` (a root prefixed `test:` is a test root; the metrics cover the other roots only). The program runs on a thread with a 512 MB stack, so deeply nested expressions are read; it parses each file once and feeds `metrics/` (the units below) and `facts/` (next entry) from the same tree. It writes `kotlin-metrics.json`: `functions` (one object per unit: `file`, `name`, `start`, `end`, `lines`, `statements`, `maxControlNesting`, `parameters`, `complexity`, `inline`, `initializer`, `lambda`), `files` (`file`, `package`, `classes`: the JVM names of its top-level classes and file facade; `imports`: the imported names, `.*` for a star import) and `types` (`metrics/Types.java`: one object per named class, interface, object and companion object, local and anonymous ones left out: `file`, `name` with its enclosing classes joined by `.`, `start`, `kind`, `methods` (member functions), `fields` (constructor `val`/`var` parameters and properties with a backing field), and `extends`, the written name of the supertype with a constructor call). `kotlin-class-limits.mjs` resolves `extends` in the project like the Java class limits (enclosing classes, single imports, same package, star imports, the name itself; an unresolved name ends the chain and counts one level) and hands the classes to the shared `classLimitFindings` (`shared/rules/size/class-limits.mjs`, as Java and Python do), so a `data class` gets the components message of a Java record, the findings of one file are ordered largest first for a stable ratchet, and an exception is matched on the module path. A syntax error exits the program with the file, line and pinned version (the sources compiled, so the compiler is too old). It builds a `KotlinCoreEnvironment` and needs an extension storage registered on the configuration (`FrontendConfigurationKeysKt.setExtensionsStorage`); that is compiler-internal API, so a bump of `kotlinCompiler` in the init script is tested with `npm run test:kotlin` (it includes `kotlin-metrics.e2e.test.mjs`, which pins the compiler on its own); `.github/workflows/kotlin-e2e.yml` runs the whole `npm run test:kotlin` suite (about 15 minutes) on a push to `main` that changes `languages/java/**`, `core/**`, `shared/**`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `vitest.config.mts` or the workflow, by hand (`workflow_dispatch`), and on a pull request when the `run-ci` label is added. `kotlin-metrics.mjs` turns `functions` into structure findings (the Java path, `structureFindings`) and into CRAP findings (`ingestCrapFunctions` with `selectKotlinMethods`, which matches a function's JaCoCo methods by first line within its range, skipping `$default`, methods inside a unit of their own and the bridge a `@JvmStatic` function leaves in the outer class; `inline` units get no CRAP score; an `initializer` unit (an `init` block, named `Class.<init>` (several blocks of a class share the name; the structure ratchet and the CRAP identity, through `numberedAmongSameSource`, number a finding only among the blocks of that name and source text that are over the same limit, so no other block, under the limit or of other text, and no order of them renames it), or a property initializer that is not a lambda or a call with a lambda but branches or holds braces of its own) runs inside `<init>` or `<clinit>`, whose JaCoCo method starts outside the unit, so `ingestCrapFunctions` joins it through `lineScoped`: the per-line counters of the file's `<sourcefile>` (`ingest/coverage-reports/jacoco-lines.mjs`, one pseudo-method per line) inside its range and outside the units nested in it, through the same `selectKotlinMethods`; the JaCoCo file is found by `package/FileName.kt`, not by directory; a `lambda` unit (a lambda, an anonymous function or a local function, named `Owner.fn.<lambda>` or after the local function or property, `Owner.property` for a property whose initializer or delegate is a call with a lambda) is joined to the JaCoCo method that starts in its range and outside the units nested in it, and, when the compiler inlined it so that no method of its own exists (`run { }`, `map { }`), through `lineFallback` to the per-line counters of its range; the function around an inlined lambda is then scored without the covered and total lines of that lambda, unless that would leave nothing to measure (`languages/java/ingest/units/unit-coverage.mjs`)), and `files` into the module map `analyzeJavaCoupling` takes (`moduleOf`, `fileOf`).
- **Kotlin facts.** The facts side of `kotlin-psi` (package `facts`) writes `kotlin-facts.json`: per file `imports`, `classes`, `functions`, `properties`, `typeAliases`, `calls`, `waits` and `predicates` (comparison chains and membership tests) with the frame chain around each (class, object, function, lambda, loop or foreach, init), absent fields omitted. For main sources the `load` package adds `loads` (each call or property access that runs while the file facade, an `object`, a companion object, an enum class or a class its companion creates loads: `name`, `call`, `receiver` as a list of `{name, call, arguments}` segments or `{opaque: true}`, `arguments`, `line`, the clipped `text` and `context` `{kind: file | object | companion | enum | singleton, owner, member}`; a function such code calls counts too, with `member` `name()` and `site`, the member of the first call; `Callees` resolves the call to a function of the enclosing classes, objects and companions (innermost first) or of the file's top level, and `Fits` checks the arguments against its parameters: count, named arguments, defaults, varargs and the type of a literal argument. `Creations` lists the argument lists with which an enum's entries or a companion's property initializers and `init` blocks create the class, directly or through a companion function they call, outside lambdas, getters and delegates; a secondary constructor is load time only when one of them selects it over the primary constructor, or a selected secondary constructor delegates to it) and `bindings` (every property, and every local `val` inside load-time code, with `name`, `owner`, `local`, the declared `type` and the `init` chain; a `this.` root is dropped), which `review-rules/main/load-effects.mjs` resolves through imports into the effect tables of `load-effect-types.mjs`; a file too deep to collect (a `StackOverflowError`, or the `PatternSyntaxException` "Stack overflow during pattern compilation" a regex compile throws at the same depth; the program compiles its patterns once, up front) is listed as `skipped` and ignored by the Node side. A predicate outside a function is named after the member or top-level property whose initializer holds it. `readKotlinFindings` passes the module and the list of all modules to `readKotlinReviewFindings` (`adapters/kotlin/review-rules/review-findings.mjs`), which runs the seven rules (`review-rules.mjs` lists them) and the Spring rules and returns findings with tool `pmd` and the Java rule ids (`unused-option` with tool `structure`); `KOTLIN_REVIEW_EXCEPTION_RULES` (`kotlin-options.mjs`) makes the `pmd/<Rule>` exceptions valid for the Kotlin preset. Test fakes, enums and forwarding targets are looked up in the facts of every module, call sites and everything else in the module's own. The sleep and timed-wait messages come from `messages/wait-advice.mjs` (`timedWaitAdvice`, `AWAITILITY_ADVICE`, `hasAwaitility`), shared with the Java rule. The program's files stay under 300 lines each. Tests feed facts as JSON (`review-rules/test-support/facts.mjs`) and `kotlin-metrics.e2e.test.mjs` runs the program.
- **Kotlin Spring rules.** The `bodies` package of `kotlin-psi` adds, for main files only, the call sites, assignments, catches and returns, each tied to its enclosing scope: `scopes` (each frame of the chain with an `id` and its `parent`, so a call is tied to its class, function and lambdas; a lambda also has its host call's `qualifier`, first non-lambda `argument`, `line` and `params` (`name`, declared `type`, and `component` for the n-th name of a destructuring), a `for` loop its `params` and `range`), `invocations` (name, receiver, arguments, `previous` and `after` in the call chain, `statement`/`returned`/`assignedTo`; `map[k] = v`, `+=` and property assignments are recorded as `set`, `plusAssign` and an `assign` call, and an array read `env["key"]` as a `get` with `index: true`; `throwing` when a lambda argument throws, `afterThrowing` (the later calls of the chain with such a lambda) and `elvis` (the right side of a `?:` around the chain)), `catches` (type, and whether the block throws), `throws` (each `throw` with its type, and `caught` when it sits in the `try` block of a `try` with catches), `locals` (each local `val`/`var`: name, scope, line, declared `type`, `initializer` callee and `alias`, the name, `this` or dotted name it copies; each entry of a destructuring statement `val (a, b) = ...` too, with its `component`), `literals` (local `val`s holding a string literal), `nullReturns` and `exceptionUses` (each use of an exception handler's parameter or of a local derived from it: the path read, whether it is in a template, a concatenation or an argument, and the calls around it; the handler is any function annotated with `@ExceptionHandler` under its simple name or an import alias of it, from `State.namesOf`). A block "throws" when it holds a `throw`, a call to `error` or `TODO`, or a call to a function declared `: Nothing` anywhere in the main sources: `KotlinPsi` parses every file first and `bodies/Throwing.nothingFunctions` collects those names before any facts are written. Declarations gain `annotationEntries` (with the annotation `type` and its use-site `target`, such as `field` or `get`, also for an entry in a `@field:[...]` group; secondary constructors too), `superClass`, `genericSupers` (supertypes written with type arguments, for `AttributeConverter<X, ...>`), `constructorAnnotations`, a parameter's `defaultValue`, a function's `expressionBody`, `returns` (the parameter or name an expression body is) and, for an expression body that is a call, `typeArgumentList`, a property's `initializer` callee, `literal` and `computed` (a getter with a body and no backing field). Annotation argument text is not clipped, so a long `@Query` keeps its whole text. `review-rules/spring/view/` builds one view per file: `spring-view.mjs` (name resolver, scope chain, member lookup including `companion object` properties), `project-types.mjs` (the project's classes, repository and enum detection through Kotlin interfaces and Java sources, `metaAnnotated` up to three levels for project annotations the all-open plugin opens) and `local-types.mjs` (`typeOf` from parameters, lambda and loop parameters, locals, properties, the implicit receiver of `apply`/`run`/`with` lambdas and extension functions, and member chains; a local from `groupBy` is a `Map<Any, List<Any>>`, each side of a destructured `partition` a `List<Any>`, and `values`/`keys` of a map a `Collection` of its value or key type; `receiverOf`, which maps `it` in `let`/`also`/`takeIf`/`takeUnless` to the call's qualifier; `declaresLocally`, which finds a local or parameter shadowing a property). `spring-scopes.mjs` holds the setup, startup and locked scopes, `isSelfCall` (no receiver, or `this`, and no implicit receiver of another object), `acceptsArguments` (a call's argument count against required, default and vararg parameters) and `returnTypeOf` (declared, or inferred from an expression body), and `rules/`, `web/` and `data/` (JPA entities, repository queries and the loop rules, with `loop-scopes.mjs` deciding what is a loop: an iterating lambda's host call is found by host name, parent scope and receiver text, and a chain is followed back through each call's `previous` to a receiver typed as a collection) the rules, each a function from a view to `{ rule, line, symbol }`; `spring-findings.mjs` gates them on `manifest.spring`, adds the messages (the allowed form only; `findingFactory` in `review-finding.mjs` appends the shared exception route, as for the Java rules) and drops `kotlin-source/HttpWithoutTimeout` and `IoCallInLoop` where a Spring client or Spring Data loop rule reports the same line (`withoutCoveredSourceFindings`, applied in `readKotlinFindings` with the config exceptions and module path from `context.classLimits`, so a Spring finding an exception lifts does not hide it). `spring-names.mjs` resolves a star-imported name to the project class when the star's package declares it, and a Kotlin or `java.lang` builtin name only to the default imports. Every message starts with a verb that names the allowed form; `adapters/spring/test-support/allowed-form.mjs` lists those verbs and the Kotlin and Java ruleset tests check each message against it. The init script writes `manifest.kotlinAllOpen` from `compileKotlin.pluginOptions` of the `org.jetbrains.kotlin.allopen` plugin. `fixtures/kotlin-spring-facts.json` is the facts file of `fixtures/kotlin-spring-project`; after changing the program or the fixture, regenerate it by running `KotlinPsi` (`java -cp <kotlin-compiler-embeddable and its dependencies>:<compiled kotlin-psi> nitpicker.kotlinpsi.KotlinPsi <metrics out> languages/java/adapters/fixtures/kotlin-spring-facts.json languages/java/adapters/fixtures/kotlin-spring-project src/main/kotlin`) or by copying `build/nitpicker/kotlin-facts.json` from a Gradle run of the project with the init script; the Kotlin e2e test fails when the findings of the recording no longer match those of the build.
- **Duplicates in every language.** `core/tooling/jscpd/` holds what the languages share: `run-jscpd.mjs` (`runJscpd`, `jscpdVersion`), `jscpd-clones.mjs` (report to neutral clones), `skipped-folders.mjs` (the per-language default lists and `duplicates.skippedFolders` replacing them; TypeScript and JavaScript also rewrite `jscpd.json` through `layoutJscpdConfig`) and `import-only.mjs`. jscpd reports whole lines, so the last line of a fragment is where the clone ends, often the line that starts the next declaration; `importsOnly(fragment, format)` treats the clone as imports only when every line is an `import`/`package` line (a leading partial name or the tail of a multi-line import are accepted; TypeScript and JavaScript also take `export ... from` re-exports; Python also takes `__all__` lists and needs at least one real import line, so a table of numbers or `import os; run()` is code). The last line is forgiven only when it starts a declaration (`class`, `def`, `fun`, `function`, `export`, an annotation or decorator, a comment and the like in that language); any other last line counts, so imports plus one real statement are reported. The duplicate scan of the base snapshot always uses the head's `duplicates` setting (`baseConfigCoveringHead` in `core/policy-changes/base-coverage.mjs`), so widening the scan never turns a legacy clone into a new one. The TypeScript `normalizeDuplicates`, the Java/Kotlin `readDuplicateFindings` and the Python one all drop such clones. The skipped-folder list reaches Java, Kotlin and Python as `--duplicate-skipped-folders <json array>` (part of the analyzer's configuration hash) and TypeScript and JavaScript through the head policy file (`duplicates`).
- **Kotlin clones.** `runJavaDuplicates(projectRoot, { format: 'kotlin', rootsKey: 'kotlinSourceRoots', reportFile: 'kotlin-duplicates.json', renamedExtensions: ['.kt', '.kts'] })` after the Java run, writing `kotlin-duplicates.json` and, from the normalized copy of the `.kt` and `.kts` files, `renamed-kotlin-duplicates.json`; both are read with `readDuplicateFindings` (given the project root) and the same rule and tool name `duplicates`.
- **detekt and ktlint SARIF.** No fingerprints, so the glue sets `nitpicker/subject` from the message text with every whole number replaced by `#` (`\b\d+\b`: a name ending in digits keeps its digits), so a measured value does not change identity, and keeps the SARIF levels (`note` stays descriptive, unlike SpotBugs). Rule ids are kept in detekt's form `detekt.<ruleset>.<Rule>`.
- **Formatter detection** is a text search of the root project's build files (not Gradle API introspection), so it works for plugin ids, version catalogs and Spotless alike. Comments are removed first and a Spotless `kotlinGradle { }` block is cut out (its formatter formats the build scripts, not the Kotlin sources); a string literal that names the other tool still matches. `ktlint` in the build files beats `ktfmt`, and `ktlint_` keys in `.editorconfig` count only when the build files name neither.
- **Source directories** are the Kotlin plugin's main `srcDirs` that exist, are inside the project and are not under the build directory (generated code). `kotlinFiles` and `kotlinSourceRoots` stay main-only (structure, CRAP, duplicates and coverage read main code); detekt, ktlint and ktfmt read `kotlinLintedDirs` (main plus the `test` source set's directories, `kotlinTestSourceRoots` in the manifest), and the detekt classpath adds the test compile classpath and the `compileTestJava` output (the main classes are dropped from it, because `analyzedClassDirs` carries them). `readSuppressionFindings` reads the same roots, so an inline suppression is banned in tests too, except a compiler-warning token (`UNCHECKED_CAST`, `DEPRECATION`, ...), which is banned on `kotlinSourceRoots` only, because the kotlinc lint compiles main sources only. Detekt's default config excludes 15 rules from `**/test/**`; the owned rules in `nitpicker-detekt.yml` reset `excludes: []` and so stay on in tests, and `TooGenericExceptionCaught` is owned (ruleset `exceptions`, with the exception names spelled out so that the default does not need the test exclusion) so that the broad-catch ban applies to tests as it does to Java (#224). A legacy test finding is not new because the head's init script runs on both snapshots.
- **detekt config validation** is switched off by a second `--config` file (`languages/java/detekt/detekt-validation.yml`), listed after the project's and Nitpicker's, because the CLI exits with code 3 on rulesets of plugins it does not load.
- **Missing reports.** A build where no project applies the plugin writes an empty `modules.json`; `readReportModules` turns that into an error naming the plugin, and a listed project without reports into one naming the project (both presets).
- **Branch coverage** (`changed-branch-coverage`) reads the source text of every file under `jacocoSourceRoots` (Java roots plus `kotlinSourceRoots`). A `.kt` line is dropped when `isCompilerGeneratedBranchLine` (`kotlin-branches.mjs`) finds a suspension call and nothing that can branch in its code (string literals, comments and type arguments removed), or when its branch count (`mb + cb`) equals `libraryBranchCount`, the sum of the measured counts of the `@InlineOnly` calls on it (`.orEmpty()` 2, `.isNullOrEmpty()` 4, `.isNullOrBlank()` 4, `LIBRARY_BRANCHES`; a `fun` declaring that name is not a call); every other line is kept, since the JaCoCo XML has branch counts per line and no instruction detail and a statement that spans lines is reported on the line of its operand. A kept line with a suspension call or one of those library calls gets the `arms` hint in its message (for a library call, that a test passing null takes its null arm). The Java preset reads `.kt` files of a Maven build through the same `ingestJacocoBranches`, so the filters apply there too. Report files are located by `sourceLocator` (`kotlin-reports.mjs`): by directory first, then, for `.kt`, by file name and `package` declaration; the same lookup resolves PIT's `sourceFile`, and a `.kt` file with missed branches that is not found (outside `kotlin`/`kotlinx`) is a descriptive `branch-source-not-found`. The measurement behind it is in `docs/kotlin-preset.md`.
- **Mutation.** `pitFindings` resolves a class's source file under `jacocoSourceRoots`. With the Kotlin preset it passes `ingestPit` a `generated` option, `kotlinGeneratedFilter` (`adapters/reporting/kotlin-reports.mjs`), which reads `kotlin-mutation-facts.json` from the module's report directory and returns `createGeneratedFilter(facts)` of `ingest/kotlin-mutants/`: a factory that `groupMutants` calls once with the mutants of the report and that returns `(mutant, filePath) => rule name or undefined`. The factory builds `siblings` (the class and method of every mutant of the report, for `default-impls-twin`) from the mutants it is given and keeps no other state, so a report is judged the same however often it is read. A mutant it names is left out of the findings and the status counts and counted in the descriptive `mutation-summary` metric `GeneratedCode`; the Java preset passes no `generated` option. `rules.mjs` holds the rules (`SOURCE_FREE_RULES` need only PIT's class, method and description and apply to `.kt` files and to unresolved paths, never to a `.java` file of a mixed module; `SOURCE_RULES` also use the facts of the source file), each a function of `{ mutant, file, cls, declared, siblings }`; `member-rules.mjs` and `mutant-kinds.mjs` hold the accessor and member rules and the mutant predicates, `facts.mjs` checks the shape of the report and fails with the missing keys. The names and the mutants each drops are in `docs/kotlin-preset.md`. Kotlin `mutant-survived` and `mutant-nocoverage` are enforceable like Java's, with the function-scoped exception sentence. `languages/java/adapters/test-support/kotlin-psi/kotlin-psi-program.mjs` compiles and runs the program in the e2e tests.
- **Kotlin mutation facts.** `languages/java/gradle/kotlin-psi/mutation/` writes `kotlin-mutation-facts.json` beside the facts path the program is given, from the same parse of the main sources (the test roots are left out, PIT does not mutate them): `{ "files": [ ... ] }`, one entry per main file, lines 1-based. `file` (path from the project directory), `package`, `lines` (the number of lines; a mutant past it comes from an inlined body of another file), `classes` (every named class, object and interface, nested ones with `$` in the JVM name, and the class of the top-level declarations: `name`, `kind`, `start`, `headerEnd`, `init` (an `init` block or a secondary constructor with a body), `serializable`, `functions` (names written in the body), `properties` (`name`, `start`, `end`, `constructor`, `getter` and `setter` written with a body, `delegated`)), `functions` (every named function and every accessor with a body: `name`, `jvmName` when `@JvmName` renames it, `class`, `start`, `end`, `suspend`, `inline` (also `inline` accessors and properties), `returnsUnit`, `inlineCalls` (names of the project's inline functions it calls), `staticIn` (the class that gets the static bridge of a `@JvmStatic` function of a companion)), `lambdas` (`start`, `end`, `valueLines`, `returnLines` (labeled returns), `callee`, `discarded` (the call's value is not used: not the last statement of a block whose value is used, through `if`, `when` and `try`), `projectCallee` (the project declares a function of that name) and `unitCallee` (every such function takes a `() -> Unit` lambda)), `branching` (lines with syntax the compiler turns into a conditional, or a call of one of the standard library's inline functions that branch or of a project inline function) and `casts` (first and last line of each `as` expression, whose null check is written code). **Build-tool contract.** The three reports of the program live in the module's report directory and their names are `KOTLIN_REPORTS` in `adapters/kotlin/report-names/kotlin-report-names.mjs`: `kotlin-metrics.json` (`{"functions":[],"files":[]}` when empty), `kotlin-facts.json` and `kotlin-mutation-facts.json` (`{"files":[]}` when empty). A build tool that runs the program passes the arguments it already passes and gets all three. A build tool that has no Kotlin files must not run it and must write the three empty values itself: Gradle does in `nitpickerKotlinPsi`, and the Maven runner (`runKotlinPsi` in `languages/java/maven/kotlin/tools/kotlin-programs.mjs` of the Kotlin-on-Maven change) must do the same in its no-Kotlin-files return. The ingest does not treat a missing mutation facts report as empty, because the gate would then count every compiler-generated mutant: `kotlinGeneratedFilter` throws an error that names the file, the directory and the empty value. `kotlin-report-contract.test.mjs` reads the Gradle block and fails when it lacks a report; the list of build tools in that test is where a new build tool adds its entry. A file that is too deep, or on which the collector fails, is skipped with a message on stderr and its mutants are judged without source facts.
- **One-way rules** (`kotlin-rules.mjs`, `nitpicker-detekt.yml`). `ONE_WAY_RULES` is the list of rules in the yml with the sentence the gate appends when detekt's text does not name the allowed form (`withAllowedForm`, after ingestion, so identity, which is the detekt text, never changes). `kotlin-messages.mjs` then ends every enforceable Kotlin finding (detekt, `kotlin-source`, `kotlinc`) with `exceptionHint` for the rule as the finding prints it (`kotlin-source/<Rule>` and `kotlinc/<rule>` carry the tool, detekt ids already name it), unless the message already names `nitpicker.config.json`; `validateKotlinOptions` accepts exactly the rules that function routes to. A rule with `mainOnly` in `ONE_WAY_RULES` counts in main sources only: `framework-allowances.mjs` drops its findings on the `kotlinTestSourceRoots` after detekt runs, not by a path glob, so its `excludes` stays `[]` (`UnusedParameter`: fakes and test doubles keep the signature they implement). `ForbiddenMethodCall` and the other rules with a `reason` field carry the allowed form in the yml instead. The yml follows the project's config in the `--config` list of the detekt task (only the validation file comes after it), and detekt gives the later file priority per key, so a project's `detekt.yml` cannot override it. Every ruleset and rule in the yml pins `active: true`, `excludes: []` and `includes: []`, and every rule `severity: warning` (the reader classifies SARIF `note` as descriptive, so a project's `severity: info` would otherwise un-gate the rule); `excludes: []` also replaces the default that skips `**/test/**` for `UnsafeCallOnNullableType`, which would skip a production package named `test`. `kotlin-rules.test.mjs` reads the yml and fails when its rules differ from `ONE_WAY_RULES` or one lacks a pin. `UseDataClass` has `allowVars: true` because `DataClassShouldBeImmutable` bans the `var` that `UseDataClass` would otherwise ask to turn into a data class. `runBlocking` is a `ForbiddenMethodCall` entry (type resolution finds the call whatever the import), so it shares the rule id, and the exception, of `run`, `with` and `also`. `ignoreAnnotated: []` is pinned too, next to `excludes: []`, because detekt skips the code of classes carrying a listed annotation for any rule. `UnusedParameter` skips annotated functions after detekt instead (`scan/parameters/declaration-header.mjs`: any annotation but `@Deprecated`, `@Suppress`, `@Throws`, `@JvmStatic`, `@JvmOverloads`, `@JvmName` and the nullability ones, as PMD does for Java), because an `ignoreAnnotated` entry would also let `@Suppress` hide the rule. The lists the yml sets (`MERGED_LISTS`: `ForbiddenMethodCall.methods`, `ForbiddenImport.imports`, `ForbiddenComment.comments`, `UseDataClass.excludeAnnotatedClasses`) are merged with the project's (detekt replaces a list): `adapters/kotlin/detekt/detekt-lists.mjs` reads the project's detekt file with the `yaml` package (a dependency, loaded lazily with `createRequire`, like `smol-toml`; the parser's alias limit and a parse failure both yield an empty list), keeps the entries that are a method name with an optional balanced parameter list (anything else could crash detekt), appends those the yml does not list and writes the result to `build/nitpicker/detekt-lists.yml`. `kotlin-tools.mjs` passes it as `-Pnitpicker.kotlin.detektLists=<absolute path>`, and the init script puts it in the `--config` list after the project's file and the yml (before the validation file); it carries only the list, so the yml still pins `active`, `severity`, `excludes`, `includes` and `ignoreAnnotated`. The file is written only when the project adds an entry, and holds only the lists it adds to. `ForbiddenComment.comments` values are regular expressions detekt matches with `containsMatchIn`; the preset's one flags a marker at the start of a comment line or before `:`/`(`, and `allowedPatterns` (one expression, not merged) skips a comment that names an issue (`#123`, `PROJ-123` but not `SHA-256`, or a link) right after the marker.
- **Type resolution classpath.** Detekt 1.23.8 embeds Kotlin 2.0.21 (it resolved a 2.1.0 standard library, not a 2.4.21 one). The stdlib it needs for `run`, `with`, `also` and `!!` is therefore `kotlin-stdlib:2.0.21` (`pinned.detektKotlin`, the version in the detekt jar's `META-INF/compiler.version`), first on the classpath, and every `kotlin-stdlib*` jar of the project is dropped. With the project's 2.4 stdlib the same run reported nothing for those calls and wrongly flagged `?.` on values of an unresolved type. `--jdk-home` is the installation path of the project's Gradle Java toolchain (`JavaPluginExtension.toolchain`, which `kotlin { jvmToolchain(n) }` also sets) when the project declares one, else the Gradle JVM's `java.home`; without it JDK types (`StringBuilder`) do not resolve. Bump `detektKotlin` together with `detekt`.
- **Exceptions.** `--exception <rule>:<glob>` (split at the first colon, one per path) reaches `applyFindingPolicy` (`shared/findings/finding-policy.mjs`), which drops findings whose rule, or `tool/rule`, is that id under the glob on both snapshots. `validateKotlinOptions` accepts any `detekt.<ruleset>.<Rule>`, `KOTLIN_LISTED_RULES` and the shared ones, so a typo is an error. Python can reuse the mechanism.
- **Inline suppression.** `kotlin-suppressions.mjs` reads the string literals inside `@Suppress`/`@SuppressWarnings` with any use-site target, after `kotlin-literals.mjs` has masked the source: one pass over comments (nested block comments), strings with `${}` templates, raw strings and character literals, which replaces each string by a numbered placeholder so that a comment opener in a string, or an annotation in a string, does not matter. `isBannedSuppression` bans every token that can target a detekt or ktlint rule: `all` in any letter case, anything starting with `detekt` or `ktlint` followed by `.`, `:` or the end, a detekt ruleset name, and a PascalCase token (every detekt rule id; detekt honors the bare id). The compiler warnings the kotlinc lint reports (`COMPILER_WARNINGS`: `DEPRECATION`, `TYPEALIAS_EXPANSION_DEPRECATION`, `UNCHECKED_CAST`, `USELESS_CAST`) are banned too, and their message routes to the `kotlinc/<rule>` exception; `unused`, `UNUSED_PARAMETER` and `UNUSED_VARIABLE` are banned in main sources only where they would hide `UnusedParameter`, which detekt honors them for: on a declaration whose parameter list is not empty, or on a class or file (`@file:Suppress`) that holds such a function (`isUnusedCodeSuppression`, `hidesUnusedParameter`); a token qualified by a rule set (`style:UnusedParameter`, `style.UnusedParameter`) is banned like the bare rule id; other compiler diagnostics are UPPER_SNAKE or lowercase and stay allowed. detekt's `ForbiddenSuppress` cannot do this alone: a suppression of `style` or `all` also hides the `ForbiddenSuppress` finding on the same annotation.
- **Source checks** (`adapters/kotlin/scan/`). `kotlin-source.mjs` scans a file once into `code` (the same length as the source, comments and string bodies blanked, newlines kept, so offsets and lines stay), `contextCode` (the same, but the code of `${...}` expressions is kept, so a literal inside a template keeps its surroundings), `literals` (start, body, `template` when it holds `$name`, `` $`name` `` or `${...}`, `raw`) and `comments` (`line`, `block`, `doc`); nested block comments and templates with braces are followed. Each rule in `scan/rules/`, `scan/security/` and `scan/literals/` takes the scanned file and returns `{ rule, offset, subject? }`; `kotlin-scan.mjs` turns them into findings (tool `kotlin-source`, identity `<rule>:<hash of subject or normalized line>` numbered per file) and runs the test-class rule on `kotlinTestSourceRoots` only. `log-calls.mjs` gives the log, catch and secret rules the log calls of `languages/java/source/loggers.mjs`, the one notion of a log call (receivers declared from a logger factory or named like a logger, every SLF4J, Log4j, JUL, kotlin-logging and Android level, the call's arguments and its lambda message), with offsets, plus `print`/`println` where a rule asks for the console. `loops.mjs` holds the loop bodies (`for`, `while`, `do` and the iterating lambdas), the retry test (`isRetry`, a `try` that leaves its loop with `return` or `break`, shared by `CatchOnlyLogsOrReturnsDefault` and `IoCallInLoop`) and the paging test of `IoCallInLoop`, Python's `io-in-loop` in Kotlin: a `while` or `do` whose condition, an `if (...) break`/`return` in it or a `?: break` reads a name assigned, over two passes, from an input or output call in the body. `catches.mjs` also exports `swallowedCatchBlocks`, the catches `CatchOnlyLogsOrReturnsDefault` reports; `readCatchLogFindings` passes them to `catchLogViolations` as `reportedElsewhere` for the files of `kotlinSourceRoots`, so a catch that only logs is reported once, as in Python. `framework-allowances.mjs` drops the detekt findings frameworks require (the environment read in a composition root, the spread into `runApplication` with `manifest.spring`), and the forbidden-API `ForbiddenMethodCall`/`ForbiddenImport` entries of the yml (every value but the scope functions, `runBlocking`, `print` and `println`) on `kotlinTestSourceRoots`, and so the one-way rules marked `mainOnly` (`UnusedParameter`), by the test roots instead of a path glob, so a `test` package in main sources is still checked. Each rule also gets a context, `{ declarations }`: `security/sql-values.mjs` collects the enum names and the declared property types of every main file of the module before the rules run, and resolves a template or concatenation piece to inert or runtime from the file's own declarations and that context. `security/` holds the sinks (`sql.mjs`, `crypto.mjs`, `clients.mjs`); a reported entry may carry its own `message` (HMAC-MD5 under `WeakHash`). The patterns that match a bare name (`TlsVerificationOff`'s insecure types, `UnsafeDeserialization`'s `JsonTypeInfo.Id.CLASS`) skip an import line (`inImport` in `kotlin-source.mjs`); the others need a call. A one-line `const val` initializer ends at the brace or `;` that closes it, so `companion object { private const val table = "users" }` reads as a literal. `literals/repeated-strings.mjs` is `RepeatedStringLiteral` (detekt's `StringLiteralDuplication` is pinned `active: false` in the yml and listed in `DISABLED_DETEKT_RULES`; it cannot exempt by context, and test data repeats literals as a rule, so only main sources are scanned): the scanned `literals` of a file without a template, five characters or more with an ASCII letter or digit, not idiomatic text, grouped by their source text; every use from the third on is reported with the text in the message and as the identity subject. The exemptions read `contextCode` through a window of 300 characters before and 100 after the literal, so the cost is linear in the file: a literal in an annotation call's parentheses (`(?<![\w`])@name(`, so `this@Outer`is not one), as a named argument, as a`to`key or the value of a`type`/`kind`/`tag`/`variant`pair, as a`["key"]`subscript, as the first argument of a translation or message-key call, or in a branch label of`when (subject) { ... }`(from the latest of the body's`{`, the previous `;`and the line start, extended over lines that end in a comma but never past the`{`, to the `->`at the body's own brace depth) is not counted. A`const val`initializer counts, as in the other languages. The idiomatic texts, translation calls, discriminant keys, minimum length and number of allowed uses are in`shared/literals/repeated-literal-exemptions.mjs`. `isConstantExpression`(a literal, a number, a CONSTANT_CASE name or a`const val`with a literal) is what`LogStringConcatenation` treats as a compile-time constant. The SQL text patterns (`STRONG_STATEMENT`, `LOOSE_STATEMENT`, `FRAGMENT_BEFORE_VALUE`) live in `shared/rules/security/sql-text.mjs`, shared with the TypeScript rule. `signatures/`holds`TriStateBoolean`, `ParameterMutation`and`CompatibilityShim`; `signatures/declarations.mjs`reads the modifiers and annotations above a declaration (including`override` or an annotation on the line before) and builds, in one pass, the enclosing brace of every offset, which the three rules share to tell a member from a local declaration and a mapped class from a plain one.
- **kotlinc lint** (`gradle/kotlin/kotlin-lint.gradle`, applied by the init script inside the Kotlin block after it sets `project.ext.nitpickerKotlinFiles` and `nitpickerKotlinJdkHome`). `nitpickerKotlinLint` runs `K2JVMCompiler` from the pinned `nitpickerKotlinCompiler` configuration with an argument file (every argument quoted), into the task's temporary directory, and writes `kotlinc.json`. The project's options come from `compileKotlin.compilerOptions` and its `pluginClasspath`/`pluginOptions`, each read in a `try`, so a Kotlin Gradle plugin without them still runs the lint, without them. `kotlin-compiler.mjs` keeps the `warning: [NAME]` lines whose name is in `COMPILER_WARNINGS` and whose file is under a Kotlin source root; a non-zero exit is one descriptive `kotlinc/lint-not-run`, since kotlinc reports no warnings once the compile has an error.
- **Fixture cases.** `test-support/kotlin-rule-cases.mjs` (next to the Kotlin rules) holds, per rule, the banned and the allowed source (`{name}` stands for the file's class name, `Banned` or `Allowed`), the detekt message recorded from the real run, and the wording the final message must contain. The CI test checks the wording against the recorded message; the end-to-end test checks the recorded message against real detekt, so a detekt or yml change that alters a message fails there.
- **Kotlin fan-out.** `UnboundedFanOut` is a review rule on the `kotlin-psi` facts (`review-rules/main/fan-out/`), printed with tool `kotlin-source` so its id and exception route stay `kotlin-source/UnboundedFanOut`. The facts give each lambda and `for` scope a `collection` (`bodies/Iterables.java`: the receiver chain of the host call or the loop range as a root, the chain links with their argument texts, and a range's operator and bounds), so a chain with lambdas (`urls.filter { }.map { }`) keeps its root, and list the top-level properties of a file as `globals` (name, declared type, initializer callee), so a client held in a top-level `val` is recognized from other files of its package or an import. `sites.mjs` finds the task starts and their per-element site and decides whether the site is fixed (builders, enum entries, windows whose size reads no size of the collection, chunk variables, count-driven ranges). `reach.mjs` follows the task body through same-class, project-class, top-level and `this::helper` calls three deep across the files of the build, with `clients.mjs` classifying a receiver by its type; a call is bounded only when a `withPermit` lambda encloses it or a semaphore `acquire()` precedes it in the same task or helper. `fanOutFindings` returns the findings and the lines of the calls they cover; `readKotlinFindings` drops `IoCallInLoop` at those lines, so a remote call has one owner and a bounded or awaited per-item call keeps the loop finding. `fixtures/kotlin-fan-out-facts.json` records the facts of `fixtures/kotlin-fan-out-project`; after a change to `kotlin-psi`, regenerate it by running `nitpicker.kotlinpsi.KotlinPsi` on that project with `src/main/kotlin` as the source root (the e2e test fails while the recording is stale).

## Python preset (`languages/python/`)

Two analyzers, `python` and `mutation` (which depends on `python`, so it runs after it and is skipped when it had a tool error, except a tool error of the tools in its `toleratedDependencyErrors`: `tests`, `dependencies`, `test-runtime`; the scheduler option is generic and validated like `reportedTools`). One runner process per snapshot and analyzer (`python-runner.mjs --part python|mutation`); the `python` part runs the tools and reads their reports from a temporary directory, the `mutation` part keeps its results in memory. The only things written into the analyzed tree are mutmut's `mutants/` directory and nitpicker's `[tool.mutmut]` table in the snapshot's `pyproject.toml` (created if absent, replacing the project's table). Reports in the temporary directory: `ruff.sarif`, `format.txt`, `coverage.json`, `coveragerc`, `manifest.json` (`formatter`, `formatterDetected`, `sourceRoots`, `testsRan`, `testFailures`, `testRunner`), `pytest-junit.xml` (xunit1), `classes.json` (the classes and import aliases of each file, for the class limits), `dependencies.json` (the declared dependencies, the distribution scan and the environment name), `type-check.json` (`checker`, `output`), `python-files.txt` (the `.py` files under the source roots, shared by the file-based steps), `rule-options.json` (`sourceRoots`, `testRunner` and `pythonFiles`, the project facts the rules script needs), `rules.json`, and `structure.json` (`languages/python/metrics/structure_metrics.py`: the limit metrics and the cyclomatic complexity). Each tool step lives in its own module (`python-test-runner.mjs`, `python-types.mjs`, `python-ast-rules.mjs`) and runs through `python-process.mjs`.

- **Environment.** That of the live checkout (`liveProjectRoot`) for the head, and for a snapshot whose dependency files equal the head's: lockfiles exist in both, but `.venv` does not exist in a work tree. `openSnapshotEnvironment` (`python-environment.mjs`, called by `createToolContext`) first asks `baseEnvironment` (`environments/base/base-environment.mjs`) for a snapshot of another root: `dependenciesDiffer` (`dependency-files.mjs`) compares `uv.lock`, `poetry.lock`, `pdm.lock`, `Pipfile`, `Pipfile.lock`, `requirements*.txt`, `constraints*.txt`, `requirements/*.txt` and the dependency tables of `pyproject.toml` (parsed with `smol-toml`; unparseable text is compared as it is), and on a difference the plan of `installers.mjs` runs in the snapshot through `execute` (`python-process.mjs`): `uv sync --frozen --no-install-project --no-install-workspace`, `poetry install --no-root --no-interaction`, `pdm sync --no-self` or `pipenv sync --dev` (each with its in-project virtualenv variable set, so the environment is `<snapshot>/.venv`), or `python3 -m venv` plus `pip install --prefer-binary -r` of a copy that `safeRequirements` (`requirements.mjs`) wrote to a temporary folder with editable, local and `file:` entries and includes outside the snapshot dropped. A `Pipfile.lock` with editable or path packages is refused, because `pipenv` has no flag to skip them. When the tool leaves no environment (a project without packages), `python3 -m venv` makes an empty one. The settings `path`, `hatch`, `conda`, a directory and an absolute path never rebuild. A missing tool raises `missingToolError` (`install-hints.mjs`, one install command per tool, also used by the head launchers). Head detection order: `python.environment`, `uv.lock`, `poetry.lock`, the `uv.lock` of an ancestor whose `pyproject.toml` has `[tool.uv.workspace]`, `Pipfile`/`Pipfile.lock` (`pipenv --venv`), `[tool.hatch.envs...]` or `hatch.toml` (`hatch env find`), `environment.yml`/`environment.yaml` (conda: its `prefix:`, else the environment `conda env list --json` lists under the file's `name:`), `.venv`, `PATH`; the code for the three managers is in `adapters/execution/environments/` (`managed.mjs` detects and asks the manager, `launchers.mjs` builds the conda launcher, `environment-error.mjs` holds the shared error), and a detected manager whose command is not installed falls through to the next row (a setting that names it errors). Nothing in that folder reads `process.env`, because the self-gate bans new environment reads outside a composition root: the conda launcher runs a tool by its path in the environment and sets `CONDA_PREFIX`, and the manager commands inherit the environment. `uv` runs `uv run --project <live> --no-sync -- <tool>` (no `--project` or no `--no-sync` would create or sync an environment) and only after `<workspace root>/.venv` (or `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT`) is seen to exist, because `uv run --no-sync` creates an empty `.venv` when there is none. Poetry cannot be used through `poetry run` because `-C` also changes the working directory of the tool, so its environment path is asked once (`poetry -C <live> env info --path`) and used like a virtualenv. A virtualenv launcher resolves a tool in its `bin` and otherwise leaves it to the `PATH` (`bin` first).
- **Snapshot sources.** `PYTHONPATH` starts with the snapshot's import roots (a source root without `__init__.py` imports from itself, a package from its parent), then the inherited value; the tools run with the snapshot as working directory. This is the only thing that makes base tests import base code when the environment has an editable install of head; it is tested for real in the e2e test.
- **Version and cache key.** `resolveVersion` runs `--version` of ruff, black (when it is the formatter) and coverage plus the pytest and mutmut import probes (`importlib.metadata` for mutmut, because `mutmut --version` fails where mutmut cannot guess its source directory) in the live environment, and throws only when the environment itself is unusable (the scheduler reports that as a tool error of each analyzer on each snapshot, with no rule, and does not run it); a missing tool reads `tool@missing` and fails in the runner. Each probe is remembered per memo (`context.probes`, created on first use), keyed by the launched command, its arguments, the working directory and the context environment. `createPythonAnalyzers` hands one memo to its `python` and `mutation` analyzers, so base and head resolve once and a type checker choice is not probed twice (`createPythonAnalyzer` and `createMutationAnalyzer` alone each keep their own); every snapshot context of a runner has its own memo.
